Multivariate Models API

Multi-equation models: VAR, VECM, BVAR (including time-varying-parameter and mixed-frequency variants), Local Projections, factor models, FAVAR, and Structural DFM, plus innovation accounting (IRF/FEVD/historical decomposition), multivariate GARCH, cointegrating regression, systems of equations, and ARDL. See VAR, VECM, BVAR, Local Projections, Factor Models, FAVAR, ARDL, and Cointegrating Regression for theory and examples.

VAR Models

MacroEconometricModels.VARModel — Type
VARModel{T} <: AbstractVARModel

VAR model estimated via OLS.

Fields: Y (data), p (lags), B (coefficients), U (residuals), Sigma (covariance), aic, bic, hqic, varnames.

source
MacroEconometricModels.SVARMAModel — Type
SVARMAModel{T<:AbstractFloat} <: AbstractVARModel

Strong Structural VARMA(p, q) model with mutually and serially independent non-Gaussian structural shocks (Gouriéroux, Monfort & Renne 2020):

\[Y_t = \mu + \sum_{k=1}^{p} \Phi_k Y_{t-k} + \varepsilon_t + \sum_{j=1}^{q} \Theta_j \varepsilon_{t-j}, \qquad \varepsilon_t = C \eta_t\]

where:

  • Y_t is the $n \times 1$ vector of endogenous variables at time $t$
  • \mu is the $n \times 1$ intercept vector
  • \Phi_k are the $n \times n$ autoregressive lag coefficient matrices ($k = 1, \ldots, p$)
  • \Theta_j are the $n \times n$ moving-average lag coefficient matrices ($j = 1, \ldots, q$)
  • \varepsilon_t is the $n \times 1$ reduced-form moving-average innovation vector
  • C is the $n \times n$ nonsingular instantaneous structural impact matrix
  • \eta_t is the $n \times 1$ vector of independent unit-variance structural shocks

Fields:

  • Y::Matrix{T} — observed sample (T_obs × n)
  • p::Int — autoregressive lag order
  • q::Int — moving-average lag order
  • mu::Vector{T} — intercept vector (n)
  • Phi::Array{T,3} — AR lag matrices (n × n × p)
  • Theta::Array{T,3} — MA lag matrices (n × n × q)
  • C::Matrix{T} — structural impact matrix (n × n)
  • shocks::Matrix{T} — filtered structural shocks (T_eff × n)
  • ma_eig_re::Vector{T} — real parts of MA companion eigenvalues (n * q)
  • ma_eig_im::Vector{T} — imaginary parts of MA companion eigenvalues (n * q)
  • regime::Symbol — :fundamental, :mixed_nonfundamental, :purely_nonfundamental, or :unit_root
  • gmr_region::Symbol — GMR (2020, Fig. 6) region (:I..:V, :unit_root, or :none)
  • distributions::Vector{Symbol} — marginal shock distribution families (n)
  • dist_params::Vector{Vector{T}} — marginal shock distribution parameters (n)
  • loglik::T — total log-likelihood
  • aic::T — Akaike information criterion
  • bic::T — Bayesian information criterion
  • varnames::Vector{String} — variable names (n)
  • shock_names::Vector{String} — structural shock names (n)
  • converged::Bool — convergence indicator
  • method::Symbol — estimation or construction method tag
  • vcov::Matrix{T} — asymptotic parameter covariance matrix (k × k, empty when unestimated)
  • se::Vector{T} — parameter standard errors corresponding to coef(m) (empty when unestimated)

Virtual properties accessible via getproperty:

  • ma_eigenvalues — Complex.(m.ma_eig_re, m.ma_eig_im)
  • nonfundamental_order — count of MA companion eigenvalues strictly outside the unit disk
  • Sigma — reduced-form innovation covariance C * C'
  • residuals — alias for shocks
  • distribution — common family symbol or :heterogeneous
  • Phi_list, Theta_list — lag-matrix views as Vector{Matrix{T}}
source
MacroEconometricModels.QuantileVARModel — Type
QuantileVARModel{T<:AbstractFloat} <: AbstractVARModel

Recursive structural Quantile VAR(p) model (Chavleishvili & Manganelli 2024) estimated across quantile levels taus:

\[A_0(\tau) \, y_t = c(\tau) + \sum_{\ell=1}^{p} A_{\ell}(\tau) \, y_{t-\ell} + u_t(\tau)\]

where:

  • $y_t$ is the $n \times 1$ vector of endogenous variables at time $t$
  • $\tau \in (0, 1)$ is the quantile level
  • $A_0(\tau)$ is the $n \times n$ unit-diagonal recursive structural impact matrix in ordering coordinates
  • $c(\tau)$ is the $n \times 1$ structural intercept vector
  • $A_{\ell}(\tau)$ are the $n \times n$ structural lag coefficient matrices for $\ell = 1, \dots, p$
  • $u_t(\tau)$ is the $n \times 1$ structural quantile residual vector satisfying $Q_\tau(u_{i,t}(\tau) \mid \mathcal{F}_{i,t}) = 0$

Fields

  • A0_list::Vector{Matrix{T}} — per-quantile $n \times n$ structural impact matrices (in original variable coordinates)
  • B_list::Vector{Matrix{T}} — per-quantile $(1 + n p) \times n$ structural intercept and lag coefficient matrices
  • resid_list::Vector{Matrix{T}} — per-quantile $T_{\text{eff}} \times n$ structural quantile residual matrices
  • se_list::Vector{Matrix{T}} — per-quantile $(1 + n p) \times n$ standard error matrices for B_list
  • taus::Vector{T} — quantile levels strictly in $(0, 1)$
  • ordering::Vector{Int} — recursive Cholesky variable ordering permutation of $1:n$
  • Y::Matrix{T} — original $T_{\text{obs}} \times n$ data matrix
  • p::Int — VAR lag order
  • varnames::Vector{String} — variable names of length $n$
  • manifest::Union{ReproManifest,Nothing} — reproducibility metadata
source
MacroEconometricModels.QuantileImpulseResponse — Type
QuantileImpulseResponse{T<:AbstractFloat} <: AbstractImpulseResponse

Impulse response functions from a Quantile VAR or Quantile FAVAR model across horizons $h = 0, \dots, H - 1$ (stored at indices $1:H$).

Fields

  • values::Array{T,3} — $H \times n_{\text{vars}} \times n_{\text{shocks}}$ point responses (values[1, :, :] is impact $h = 0$)
  • ci_lower::Array{T,3} — $H \times n_{\text{vars}} \times n_{\text{shocks}}$ lower interval bounds (zeros when ci_type == :none)
  • ci_upper::Array{T,3} — $H \times n_{\text{vars}} \times n_{\text{shocks}}$ upper interval bounds (zeros when ci_type == :none)
  • horizon::Int — number of horizons $H$
  • tau_profile::Vector{T} — horizon-by-horizon quantile levels of length $H$
  • baseline_tau::T — baseline reference quantile level
  • shock_size::T — structural shock magnitude
  • method::Symbol — propagation method (:analytical, :simulation, or :vb)
  • ci_type::Symbol — uncertainty interval type (:none, :bootstrap, or :credible)
  • conf_level::T — confidence or credible interval level
  • variables::Vector{String} — response variable names
  • shocks::Vector{String} — shock names
source

Non-Fundamental SSVARMA

MacroEconometricModels.ssvarma_filter_shocks — Method
ssvarma_filter_shocks(Y, mu, Phi, Theta, C; tol=1e-8) -> NamedTuple
ssvarma_filter_shocks(Y, Phi, Theta, C; mu=nothing, tol=1e-8) -> NamedTuple
ssvarma_filter_shocks(U, Theta, C; tol=1e-8) -> NamedTuple
ssvarma_filter_shocks(m::SVARMAModel; tol=1e-8) -> NamedTuple

Recover structural shocks $\hat{\eta}_t$ and sample-average log-Jacobian across arbitrary fundamental, mixed, and purely non-fundamental SSVARMA(p, q) regimes via the real Schur forward/backward algorithm of Gouriéroux, Monfort & Renne (2020, §4.1.2, §4.1.4).

Given AR-filtered reduced-form residuals $u_t = Y_t - \mu - \sum_{k=1}^p \Phi_k Y_{t-k}$ satisfying the moving-average system $u_t = \varepsilon_t + \sum_{j=1}^q \Theta_j \varepsilon_{t-j}$ with $\varepsilon_t = C \eta_t$, the companion matrix $F_{\text{ma}}$ ($-\Theta_1$ for $q = 1$) is factored via real Schur decomposition and reordered via ordschur:

\[F_{\text{ma}} = Z T Z', \qquad T = \begin{bmatrix} T_{11} & T_{12} \\ 0 & T_{22} \end{bmatrix}, \qquad \tilde{u}_t = Z_{1:n, :}' u_t\]

where:

  • F_{\text{ma}} is the $nq \times nq$ moving-average companion matrix
  • Z is the $nq \times nq$ orthogonal Schur basis matrix partitioned into causal (|\lambda| < 1) and anti-causal (|\lambda| > 1) blocks, with top $n$ rows Z_{1:n, :}
  • T is the $nq \times nq$ reordered real Schur form
  • T_{11} is the $m_1 \times m_1$ upper quasi-triangular causal block with all eigenvalues strictly inside the unit circle
  • T_{22} is the $m_2 \times m_2$ upper quasi-triangular anti-causal block with all eigenvalues strictly outside the unit circle
  • T_{12} is the $m_1 \times m_2$ cross-coupling block
  • u_t is the $n \times 1$ AR-filtered reduced-form residual at time $t$
  • \tilde{u}_t is the rotated residual vector in Schur coordinates

Block 2 is solved backward from terminal condition $\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2, T_{\text{eff}}} = 0$, block 1 is solved forward from $\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1, 0} = 0$, and structural shocks are recovered as $\hat{\eta}_t = C^{-1} (Z \tilde{\varepsilon}_t)_{1:n}$. Per GMR (2020, Prop. 2), the exact sample-average log-Jacobian is:

\[T_{\text{eff}}^{-1} \log |\det(\partial u / \partial \eta)| = \log |\det(C)| + \sum_{| \lambda_j | > 1} \log | \lambda_j |\]

where:

  • T_{\text{eff}} is the effective sample size (T_obs - p)
  • u and \eta denote the stacked reduced-form residuals and structural shocks
  • C is the $n \times n$ structural impact matrix
  • \lambda_j are the eigenvalues of the MA companion matrix $F_{\text{ma}}$

Returns a NamedTuple (shocks, errors, residuals, log_jacobian, log_det_C, log_det_T22, eigenvalues, n_fundamental, n_nonfundamental).

source
MacroEconometricModels.blaschke_representations — Function
blaschke_representations(Theta, C=nothing; tol=1e-8) -> Vector{NamedTuple}
blaschke_representations(m::SVARMAModel; tol=1e-8) -> Vector{NamedTuple}

Enumerate all $2^r$ second-moment-equivalent real SVARMA moving-average representations $(\Theta^*(L), C^*)$ generated by flipping subsets of the $r$ nonzero real roots and complex-conjugate root pairs of $\det \Theta(z) = 0$ across the unit circle via Lippi–Reichlin (1994) elementary Blaschke matrices:

\[\Theta^*(z) C^* = \Theta(z) C B(z), \qquad B(e^{-i\omega}) B(e^{-i\omega})^* = I_n\]

where:

  • \Theta(z) is the $n \times n$ moving-average lag polynomial $I_n + \sum_{j=1}^q \Theta_j z^j$
  • C is the $n \times n$ nonsingular instantaneous structural impact matrix (I_n if omitted)
  • B(z) is a real-rational para-unitary Blaschke matrix preserving the spectral density $f_u(\omega)$
  • (\Theta^*(z), C^*) is a second-moment-equivalent real moving-average representation with inverted flipped roots

Elementary Blaschke factors flip each real root $\lambda \mapsto \lambda^{-1}$ and each complex-conjugate pair $(\lambda, \bar{\lambda}) \mapsto (\lambda^{-1}, \bar{\lambda}^{-1})$ while maintaining exact degree $\le q$ and real coefficients. The first element corresponds to the unmodified input (Theta, C).

Returns a Vector of NamedTuples with fields (Theta, C, Sigma, regime, n_nonfundamental, eigenvalues, gmr_region).

source
MacroEconometricModels.fevd — Method
fevd(m::SVARMAModel{T}, H::Int=20) -> FEVD{T}

Compute structural forecast error variance decomposition for an SVARMAModel over horizons $1, \ldots, H$ from the causal structural impulse responses $\Psi_h C$.

Because structural shocks $\eta_t$ are mutually and serially independent with unit variance, rows of proportions[:, :, h] sum to 1.0 at every horizon h.

source
MacroEconometricModels.fundamentalness_regime — Function
fundamentalness_regime(Theta, C=nothing; tol=1e-8) -> NamedTuple
fundamentalness_regime(m::SVARMAModel; tol=1e-8) -> NamedTuple

Classify the moving-average polynomial $\Theta(L) = I_n + \sum_{j=1}^q \Theta_j L^j$ by the location of its companion matrix eigenvalues relative to the unit circle:

\[F_{\text{ma}} = \begin{bmatrix} -\Theta_1 & -\Theta_2 & \cdots & -\Theta_q \\ I_n & 0 & \cdots & 0 \\ \vdots & \ddots & \ddots & \vdots \\ 0 & \cdots & I_n & 0 \end{bmatrix}\]

where:

  • F_{\text{ma}} is the $nq \times nq$ moving-average companion matrix
  • \Theta_j are the $n \times n$ moving-average lag matrices ($j = 1, \ldots, q$)
  • I_n is the $n \times n$ identity matrix

Classifies regime as:

  • :fundamental — all eigenvalues satisfy $|\lambda_i| < 1 - \text{tol}$
  • :mixed_nonfundamental — both $|\lambda_i| < 1 - \text{tol}$ and $|\lambda_i| > 1 + \text{tol}$ eigenvalues exist
  • :purely_nonfundamental — all eigenvalues satisfy $|\lambda_i| > 1 + \text{tol}$
  • :unit_root — at least one eigenvalue lies within tol of the unit circle (||\lambda_i| - 1| \le \text{tol})

For bivariate VMA(1) systems (n == 2 && q == 1), additionally assigns gmr_region to Gouriéroux, Monfort & Renne (2020, Fig. 6, Appendix B.1) regions :I..:V in the $(\text{tr}(-\Theta_1), \det(-\Theta_1))$ plane separated by $1 \mp \text{tr} + \det = 0$ and $\det = 1$ (:unit_root on boundaries, :none otherwise).

Returns (regime, n_fundamental, n_nonfundamental, eigenvalues, gmr_region).

source
MacroEconometricModels.irf — Method
irf(m::SVARMAModel{T}, H::Int=20) -> ImpulseResponse{T}

Compute causal structural impulse responses for an SVARMAModel over horizons $h = 0, \ldots, H - 1$ (stored at indices 1:H):

\[\Psi(L) = \Phi(L)^{-1} \Theta(L) = \sum_{h=0}^{\infty} \Psi_h L^h, \qquad \mathrm{IRF}_h = \Psi_h C\]

where:

  • \Phi(L) is the autoregressive matrix polynomial $I_n - \sum_{k=1}^p \Phi_k L^k$
  • \Theta(L) is the moving-average matrix polynomial $I_n + \sum_{j=1}^q \Theta_j L^j$
  • \Psi_h is the $n \times n$ reduced-form Wold-type multiplier at horizon $h$
  • C is the $n \times n$ structural impact matrix (\mathrm{IRF}_0 = C)
source
MacroEconometricModels.estimate_ssvarma — Method
estimate_ssvarma(Y::AbstractMatrix, p::Int, q::Int=1;
                 method::Symbol=:pml, distribution::Symbol=:student_t,
                 k_iv::Int=p+3, se::Symbol=:auto, max_iter::Int=500,
                 tol::Real=1e-6, n_starts::Int=3,
                 varnames=nothing, shock_names=nothing,
                 rng=Random.default_rng(), seed=nothing) -> SVARMAModel{T}

Estimate a non-Gaussian Strong Structural VARMA(p, q) model following Gouriéroux, Monfort & Renne (2020):

\[Y_t = \mu + \sum_{k=1}^{p} \Phi_k Y_{t-k} + \varepsilon_t + \sum_{j=1}^{q} \Theta_j \varepsilon_{t-j}, \qquad \varepsilon_t = C \eta_t\]

where:

  • Y_t is the $n \times 1$ vector of endogenous observables at time $t$
  • \mu is the $n \times 1$ intercept vector
  • \Phi_k are the $n \times n$ autoregressive lag matrices ($k = 1, \ldots, p$)
  • \Theta_j are the $n \times n$ moving-average lag matrices ($j = 1, \ldots, q$)
  • C is the $n \times n$ structural impact matrix normalized by GMR Assumption A.7
  • \eta_t are mutually and serially independent zero-mean unit-variance non-Gaussian shocks

Two estimation procedures (method) are supported:

  • :pml (default) or :ml — maximizes the GMR (2020, eq. 4.16) truncated log-likelihood with non-fundamental Schur Jacobian $-T_{\text{eff}} (\log |\det C| + \sum_{|\lambda_i(\Theta)| > 1} \log |\lambda_i(\Theta)|)$ across every Blaschke root-flipped regime basin (blaschke_representations), initialized from 2SLS-GMM and marginal shock MLE.
  • :gmm — two-step semi-parametric 2SLS-GMM estimator (GMR §4.2): Step 1 estimates $(\mu, \Phi_1, \ldots, \Phi_p)$ by 2SLS with lagged instruments $Y_{t-q-1}, \ldots, Y_{t-q-k_{\text{iv}}}$; Step 2 estimates $(C_0, \ldots, C_q, \kappa_3, \kappa_4)$ from order-2/3/4 cumulant moments across non-symmetric direction pairs with exact first-step-error-adjusted Newey–West HAC optimal weighting $\Omega^* = Q_0^{-1}$ and nonparametric KDE shock densities.

Keyword Arguments

  • method::Symbol=:pml — :pml, :ml, or :gmm
  • distribution::Symbol=:student_t — marginal density for :pml/:ml (:student_t, :mixture_normal_asym, :mixture_normal, :pearson_iv, :pml, :skew_normal)
  • k_iv::Int=p+3 — number of lagged instrument lags for Step 1 2SLS
  • se::Symbol=:auto — standard error type (:auto selects :sandwich for :pml, :hessian for :ml, first-step-adjusted GMM covariance for :gmm; :opg also supported)
  • max_iter::Int=500 — maximum optimizer iterations per regime start
  • tol::Real=1e-6 — gradient convergence tolerance
  • n_starts::Int=3 — additional orthogonal rotations per regime for multi-start search
  • varnames — optional length-n vector of variable names
  • shock_names — optional length-n vector of structural shock names
  • rng::AbstractRNG=Random.default_rng() — random number generator
  • seed::Union{Integer,Nothing}=nothing — optional seed overriding rng

References

  • Gouriéroux, C., Monfort, A., & Renne, J.-P. (2020). Identification and Estimation in Non-Fundamental Structural VARMA Models. Review of Economic Studies, 87(4), 1915–1953.
source

VECM Models

VECMModel stores estimated Vector Error Correction Model parameters ($\alpha$, $\beta$, $\Gamma$, $\mu$, $\Sigma$), cointegrating rank, and test diagnostics. It supports the dynamic property m.eigenvalues, which delegates to m.johansen_result.eigenvalues to expose the ordered eigenvalues from Johansen reduced-rank regression.

VECMRestrictionTest holds log-likelihood ratio test statistics, degrees of freedom, and refitted models under restrictions on $\beta$ (test_beta_restriction), $\alpha$ (test_alpha_restriction), weak exogeneity (test_weak_exogeneity), known $\beta$ (test_known_beta), and joint restrictions (test_joint_restriction). For Johansen Cases 2 and 4, restrictions on $\beta$ automatically embed into $(n+1)$-space via $H^* = [H \; 0; 0 \; 1]$, leaving restricted deterministic terms unconstrained and preserving $\mathrm{df} = r(n-s)$.

MacroEconometricModels.VECMModel — Type
VECMModel{T} <: AbstractVARModel

Vector Error Correction Model estimated via Johansen MLE or Engle-Granger two-step.

The VECM representation: ΔYₜ = αβ'Yₜ₋₁ + Γ₁ΔYₜ₋₁ + ... + Γₚ₋₁ΔYₜ₋ₚ₊₁ + μ + uₜ

where Π = αβ' is the long-run matrix (n × n, rank r), α (n × r) are adjustment speeds, and β (n × r) are cointegrating vectors.

Fields

  • Y: Original data in levels (T_obs × n)
  • p: Underlying VAR order (VECM has p-1 lagged differences)
  • rank: Cointegrating rank r
  • alpha: Adjustment coefficients (n × r)
  • beta: Cointegrating vectors (n × r), Phillips-normalized
  • Pi: Long-run matrix αβ' (n × n)
  • Gamma: Short-run dynamics [Γ₁, ..., Γₚ₋₁]
  • mu: Intercept (n)
  • U: Residuals (T_eff × n)
  • Sigma: Residual covariance (n × n)
  • aic, bic, hqic: Information criteria
  • loglik: Log-likelihood
  • deterministic: :none, :constant, or :trend
  • method: :johansen or :engle_granger
  • johansen_result: Johansen test result (if applicable)
source
MacroEconometricModels.VECMForecast — Type
VECMForecast{T}

Forecast result from a VECM, preserving cointegrating relationships.

Fields

  • levels: Forecasts in levels (h × n)
  • differences: Forecasts in first differences (h × n)
  • ci_lower, ci_upper: Confidence interval bounds in levels (h × n)
  • horizon: Forecast horizon
  • ci_method: CI method used (:none, :bootstrap, :simulation)
  • conf_level: Confidence level for CIs (e.g., 0.95)
source
MacroEconometricModels.VECMGrangerResult — Type
VECMGrangerResult{T}

VECM Granger causality test result with short-run, long-run, and strong (joint) tests.

Fields

  • short_run_stat, short_run_pvalue, short_run_df: Wald test on Γ coefficients
  • long_run_stat, long_run_pvalue, long_run_df: Wald test on α (error correction)
  • strong_stat, strong_pvalue, strong_df: Joint test
  • cause_var, effect_var: Variable indices
source
MacroEconometricModels.VECMRestrictionTest — Type
VECMRestrictionTest{T}

Johansen likelihood-ratio test of a linear restriction on the cointegrating structure (β and/or α) of an estimated VECMModel.

The statistic compares the restricted and unrestricted reduced-rank likelihoods,

LR = T Σ_{i=1}^{r} ln[(1 − λ*_i)/(1 − λ_i)]        (β / α restrictions)

where λ_i are the unrestricted eigenvalues and λ*_i the eigenvalues of the transformed eigenproblem, distributed asymptotically χ²(df) under H₀.

Fields

  • kind: :beta, :alpha, :weak_exogeneity, :known_beta, or :joint
  • lr_stat: LR test statistic
  • df: χ² degrees of freedom
  • pvalue: asymptotic p-value
  • rank: cointegrating rank r
  • description: human-readable restriction description
  • beta_restricted: re-normalized β under H₀ (n × r)
  • beta_unrestricted: β of the input model (n × r)
  • eigenvalues_restricted: restricted eigenvalues λ₁,…,λ_r (empty for :joint)
  • eigenvalues_unrestricted: unrestricted eigenvalues λ₁,…,λ_r
  • converged: whether estimation converged (always true for closed-form tests; reflects the switching-algorithm status for :joint)
  • restricted_model: re-estimated VECMModel imposing H₀, so downstream irf/fevd/historical_decomposition run on the restricted system
source

Analysis Result Types

MacroEconometricModels.AbstractAnalysisResult — Type
AbstractAnalysisResult

Abstract supertype for all innovation accounting and structural analysis results. Provides a unified interface for accessing results from various methods (IRF, FEVD, HD).

Subtypes should implement:

  • point_estimate(result) - return point estimate
  • has_uncertainty(result) - return true if uncertainty bounds available
  • uncertainty_bounds(result) - return (lower, upper) bounds if available
source
MacroEconometricModels.AbstractFrequentistResult — Type
AbstractFrequentistResult <: AbstractAnalysisResult

Frequentist analysis results with point estimates and optional confidence intervals.

source
MacroEconometricModels.AbstractBayesianResult — Type
AbstractBayesianResult <: AbstractAnalysisResult

Impulse Response and FEVD

MacroEconometricModels.ImpulseResponse — Type
ImpulseResponse{T} <: AbstractImpulseResponse

IRF results with optional confidence intervals.

Fields: values (H×n×n), cilower, ciupper, horizon, variables, shocks, citype. Internal: _draws (raw bootstrap/simulation draws for correct cumulative IRF), _conflevel. manifest::Union{ReproManifest,Nothing} is set by the bootstrap path when seed= is passed; reproduce(ir, model) re-runs from that seed. Deterministic IRFs store nothing.

Bayesian IRF with posterior quantiles.

Fields: quantiles (H×n×n×q), pointestimate (H×n×n), horizon, variables, shocks, quantilelevels. Internal: _draws (raw posterior draws for correct cumulative IRF).

MacroEconometricModels.QuantileFactorModel — Type
QuantileFactorModel{T<:AbstractFloat} <: AbstractFactorModel

Quantile factor model (Chen, Dolado & Gonzalo 2021) estimated at quantile tau via Iterative Quantile Regression (:iqr) or Convolution-Smoothed QFA (:smoothed_iqr).

Fields

  • X::Matrix{T} — raw data panel (T_obs × N)
  • tau::T — fitted quantile level in (0, 1)
  • factors::Matrix{T} — normalized quantile factors (T_obs × r, F'F / T_obs = I_r)
  • loadings::Matrix{T} — normalized quantile loadings (N × r, Λ'Λ / N = diag(sigma_sq))
  • sigma_sq::Vector{T} — factor strengths (diag(Λ'Λ / N), descending)
  • common_component::Matrix{T} — fitted common component (T_obs × N, in raw X units)
  • residuals::Matrix{T} — X - common_component (in raw X units)
  • objective::T — average check-function loss (NT)⁻¹ Σ ρ_τ(X_proc - FΛ')
  • pseudo_r2::Vector{T} — Koenker–Machado per-series 1 - V̂_i(τ) / Ṽ_i(τ)
  • r::Int — number of quantile factors
  • method::Symbol — estimation method (:iqr or :smoothed_iqr)
  • standardized::Bool — whether X was standardized prior to estimation
  • converged::Bool — whether the winning solver run satisfied the stopping rule
  • iterations::Int — outer IQR or L-BFGS iterations performed by the winning run
  • se_loadings::Union{Nothing,Matrix{T}} — optional loading SEs (N × r) when se != :none
  • se_factors::Union{Nothing,Matrix{T}} — optional factor SEs (T_obs × r) when se != :none
  • varnames::Vector{String} — variable names (length N)
  • manifest::Union{ReproManifest,Nothing} — reproducibility metadata
source
MacroEconometricModels.QFMICResult — Type
QFMICResult{T<:AbstractFloat}

Result of quantile-specific factor count selection via ic_criteria_qfm (Chen, Dolado & Gonzalo 2021, §3.2, Theorem 2).

\[P_{NT} = c \, \hat{\sigma}_1^2(\tau) \, L_{NT}^{-2/3} \left(\log L_{NT}^2\right)^{1/3}, \qquad IC_p(k; \tau) = \log M_{NT}(\hat{\Lambda}^{(k)}, \hat{F}^{(k)}; \tau) + k \, g_p(N, T)\]

where:

  • $P_{NT}$ is the rank-minimization singular-value threshold
  • $c$ is threshold_scale (default 1.0)
  • $\hat{\sigma}_1^2(\tau)$ is the largest factor strength at r_max
  • $L_{NT} = \min(\sqrt{N}, \sqrt{T})$ is the effective panel rate
  • $M_{NT}(\cdot; \tau)$ is the average check-function loss at k factors
  • $g_p(N, T)$ for $p \in \{1, 2, 3\}$ are the three Bai–Ng (2002) penalty functions

Fields

  • tau::T — quantile level in (0, 1)
  • r_max::Int — maximum number of factors evaluated
  • sigma_sq::Vector{T} — descending factor strengths diag(Λ'Λ / N) from the r_max fit (length r_max)
  • threshold_PNT::T — CDG rank-minimization threshold P_NT
  • r_rank::Int — factor count selected by rank-minimization (count(σ_j² ≥ P_NT))
  • loss::Vector{T} — average check-loss path across k = 0:r_max (length r_max + 1; index 1 is k = 0)
  • IC1::Vector{T} — Bai–Ng check-loss IC1 values across k = 0:r_max (length r_max + 1)
  • IC2::Vector{T} — Bai–Ng check-loss IC2 values across k = 0:r_max (length r_max + 1)
  • IC3::Vector{T} — Bai–Ng check-loss IC3 values across k = 0:r_max (length r_max + 1)
  • r_IC1::Int — factor count minimizing IC1 (0 ≤ r_IC1 ≤ r_max)
  • r_IC2::Int — factor count minimizing IC2 (0 ≤ r_IC2 ≤ r_max)
  • r_IC3::Int — factor count minimizing IC3 (0 ≤ r_IC3 ≤ r_max)
source

QuantileFactorGrid{T<:AbstractFloat}

Container for a grid of QuantileFactorModel fits across quantile levels $\tau_1, \dots, \tau_M$, permitting quantile-specific factor counts $r(\tau_j)$.

Fields

  • taus::Vector{T} — quantile levels strictly in (0, 1)
  • models::Vector{QuantileFactorModel{T}} — fitted QFM objects, one per quantile
  • r_by_tau::Vector{Int} — factor count $r(\tau_j)$ at each quantile
  • varnames::Vector{String} — variable names (length N)
  • manifest::Union{ReproManifest,Nothing} — reproducibility metadata
source
MacroEconometricModels.QFMSpanningResult — Type
QFMSpanningResult{T<:AbstractFloat}

Result of comparing quantile factors $\hat{F}(\tau)$ against conditional-mean PCA factors $\hat{F}_{\text{PCA}}$ via test_qfm_spanning (Chen, Dolado & Gonzalo 2021, §5 Table III).

Fields

  • taus::Vector{T} — quantile levels evaluated
  • r_qfm::Vector{Int} — number of quantile factors at each quantile
  • r_pca::Int — number of conditional-mean PCA factors
  • ols_r2::Vector{Vector{T}} — per-quantile, per-factor OLS $R^2$ from regressing $\hat{F}_k(\tau)$ on $[1, \hat{F}_{\text{PCA}}]$
  • quantile_r1::Vector{Vector{T}} — per-quantile, per-factor Koenker–Machado $R^1(\tau)$ from quantile regression on $[1, \hat{F}_{\text{PCA}}]$
  • cancor::Vector{Vector{T}} — canonical correlations between $\hat{F}(\tau)$ and $\hat{F}_{\text{PCA}}$ (descending, length $\min(r(\tau), r_{\text{PCA}})$)
  • unspanned::Vector{Vector{Bool}} — true where ols_r2[j][k] < threshold
  • threshold::T — OLS $R^2$ threshold flagging unspanned tail factors
  • n_obs::Int — number of time-series observations $T$
  • n_vars::Int — number of cross-sectional series $N$
source
MacroEconometricModels.QFMLoadingConstancyTest — Type
QFMLoadingConstancyTest{T<:AbstractFloat} <: StatsAPI.HypothesisTest

Result of testing loading-space constancy across quantiles $\tau_1$ and $\tau_2$ via test_qfm_loading_constancy.

\[H_0: \mathrm{span}(\Lambda(\tau_1)) = \mathrm{span}(\Lambda(\tau_2)), \qquad S = \|(I - P_{\hat{\Lambda}(\tau_2)}) \hat{\Lambda}(\tau_1)\|_F^2\]

where:

  • $\Lambda(\tau_1), \Lambda(\tau_2)$ are the quantile loading matrices at $\tau_1$ and $\tau_2$
  • $P_{\hat{\Lambda}(\tau_2)}$ is the orthogonal projector onto $\mathrm{span}(\hat{\Lambda}(\tau_2))$
  • $S$ is the Frobenius-squared projection residual norm

Fields

  • tau1::T — first quantile level
  • tau2::T — second quantile level
  • r1::Int — factor rank at tau1
  • r2::Int — factor rank at tau2
  • statistic::T — observed projection residual norm squared
  • pvalue::T — bootstrap p-value (1 + #{S* ≥ S}) / (n_boot + 1)
  • boot_stats::Vector{T} — bootstrap null draws of length n_boot
  • n_boot::Int — number of bootstrap replications
  • block_length::Int — moving-block length used
  • bootstrap::Symbol — :block or :wild
  • n_obs::Int — number of time-series observations $T$
  • n_vars::Int — number of cross-sectional series $N$
  • dof::Int — nominal residual subspace degrees of freedom
  • manifest::Union{ReproManifest,Nothing} — reproducibility metadata
source
MacroEconometricModels.GrowthAtRiskResult — Type
GrowthAtRiskResult{T<:AbstractFloat}

Result of Adrian–Boyarchenko–Giannone (2019) direct quantile-regression Growth-at-Risk and conditional density estimation via growth_at_risk.

\[Q_{y_{t+h}}(\tau \mid I_t) = \alpha_h(\tau) + \beta_h(\tau)' \hat{F}_t(\tau) + \gamma_h(\tau)' y_{t:-1:t-p+1}, \qquad \mathrm{GaR}_{\alpha, t} = \hat{F}_{t+h \mid t}^{-1}(\alpha), \qquad \mathrm{ES}_{\alpha, t} = \frac{1}{\alpha} \int_0^\alpha \hat{F}_{t+h \mid t}^{-1}(u)\, du\]

where:

  • $y_{t+h}$ is the $h$-step-ahead target variable
  • $\hat{F}_t(\tau)$ is the vector of estimated quantile factors at quantile level $\tau$
  • $y_{t:-1:t-p+1}$ are the $p$ target lags (with p = lags)
  • $\hat{F}_{t+h \mid t}$ is the fitted conditional predictive distribution (:skewed_t, :normal, or empirical :none)
  • $\mathrm{GaR}_{\alpha, t}$ and $\mathrm{ES}_{\alpha, t}$ are Growth-at-Risk and Expected Shortfall at tail probability $\alpha$

Fields

  • y::Vector{T} — full target series (length n_obs)
  • target_name::String — name of the target variable
  • taus::Vector{T} — quantile levels strictly in (0, 1) (length M)
  • horizon::Int — forecast horizon h
  • lags::Int — number of target lags included in direct quantile regressions
  • alpha::T — tail probability level in (0, 1) for GaR and Expected Shortfall
  • density::Symbol — predictive density family (:skewed_t, :normal, or :none)
  • rearrange::Bool — whether Chernozhukov–Fernández-Val–Galichon (2010) rearrangement was applied
  • fitted_quantiles::Matrix{T} — in-sample fitted conditional quantiles (n_eff × M)
  • actual_target::Vector{T} — aligned in-sample realized target values y_{t+h} (length n_eff)
  • oos_quantiles::Vector{T} — out-of-sample T+h|T conditional quantile forecast across taus (length M)
  • coefficients::Vector{Vector{T}} — direct quantile regression parameter vectors per quantile (length M)
  • density_params::Matrix{T} — in-sample fitted density parameters (n_eff × 4 for :skewed_t [μ, σ, α_skew, ν], n_eff × 2 for :normal [μ, σ], 0 × 0 for :none)
  • oos_density_params::Vector{T} — T+h|T fitted density parameters (length 4, 2, or 0)
  • gar::Vector{T} — in-sample Growth-at-Risk GaR_α series (length n_eff)
  • expected_shortfall::Vector{T} — in-sample Expected Shortfall ES_α series (length n_eff)
  • upside_entropy::Vector{T} — in-sample upside relative entropy above the conditional median (length n_eff)
  • downside_entropy::Vector{T} — in-sample downside relative entropy below the conditional median (length n_eff)
  • oos_gar::T — T+h|T Growth-at-Risk forecast
  • oos_expected_shortfall::T — T+h|T Expected Shortfall forecast
  • oos_upside_entropy::T — T+h|T upside relative entropy
  • oos_downside_entropy::T — T+h|T downside relative entropy
  • quantile_score::Vector{T} — mean in-sample check-loss quantile score per quantile level (length M)
  • crps::Vector{T} — in-sample Continuous Ranked Probability Score series (length n_eff)
  • oos_split::Union{Int,Nothing} — origin index where expanding-window OOS evaluation starts (or nothing)
  • pit::Vector{T} — OOS Probability Integral Transforms F_{t+h|t}(y_{t+h}) (T[] when oos_split === nothing)
  • oos_quantile_scores::Vector{T} — mean OOS check-loss quantile score per quantile level (T[] when oos_split === nothing)
  • oos_crps::Vector{T} — OOS CRPS series (T[] when oos_split === nothing)
  • n_obs::Int — full sample length T_obs
  • n_eff::Int — effective in-sample estimation length T_eff
  • manifest::Union{ReproManifest,Nothing} — reproducibility metadata (nothing when seed === nothing)
source
MacroEconometricModels.CointegratedGDFM — Type
CointegratedGDFM{T<:AbstractFloat} <: AbstractFactorModel

Non-stationary $I(1)$ cointegrated Generalized Dynamic Factor Model estimated via the Barigozzi, Lippi & Luciani (2021) random-permutation blockwise VECM procedure:

\[X_t = \tau_t + \chi_t + \xi_t, \qquad \chi_t = \sum_{h=0}^{\infty} C_h \eta_{t-h}\]

where:

  • $X_t$ is the $N \times 1$ observed panel in levels at time $t$
  • $\tau_t$ is the deterministic component (:none, :constant, or :linear)
  • $\chi_t$ is the $I(1)$ common component in levels driven by $q$ common shocks
  • $\xi_t$ is the $I(0)$ idiosyncratic component
  • $C_h$ is the $N \times q$ level impulse response matrix at horizon $h$, converging to $C(1)$
  • $\eta_t$ is the $q \times 1$ vector of identified structural common shocks

Fields

  • X::Matrix{T}: Original panel data in levels (T_obs × N)
  • common_levels::Matrix{T}: Estimated common component in levels (T_obs × N, including deterministic trend)
  • idiosyncratic::Matrix{T}: Idiosyncratic component X - common_levels (T_obs × N)
  • irf_matrices::Array{T,3}: Identified level impulse response matrices $C_0, \dots, C_H$ ((irf_horizon + 1) × N × q)
  • long_run_impact::Matrix{T}: Long-run level impact matrix $C(1)$ (N × q, rank $n_{\text{permanent}}$)
  • B0::Matrix{T}: Structural identification rotation matrix (q × q) mapping reduced-form innovations to structural shocks
  • shocks::Matrix{T}: Identified structural shocks $\hat{\eta}_t$ (T_eff × q)
  • trend_coef::Matrix{T}: Deterministic trend coefficients [μ'; δ'] (2 × N)
  • p_blocks::Matrix{Int}: Selected block VECM VAR lag orders (n_perm × n_blocks)
  • q::Int: Number of dynamic common shocks
  • n_permanent::Int: Number of permanent stochastic trends (0 ≤ n_permanent ≤ q)
  • n_perm::Int: Number of cross-sectional permutations averaged
  • bandwidth::Int: Spectral lag-window truncation parameter M
  • kernel::Symbol: Spectral lag-window kernel (:bartlett, :parzen, or :tukey)
  • det_trend::Symbol: Deterministic trend specification (:none, :constant, or :linear)
  • identification::Symbol: Structural identification scheme (:permanent_transitory, :cholesky, :long_run, :sign, or :proxy)
  • irf_horizon::Int: Maximum stored level IRF horizon H
  • varnames::Vector{String}: Panel variable names (length N)
  • shock_names::Vector{String}: Structural shock names (length q)
  • manifest::Union{ReproManifest,Nothing}: Reproducibility manifest
source
MacroEconometricModels.BandPassGDFMResult — Type
BandPassGDFMResult{T<:AbstractFloat}

Frequency-band decomposition of a GeneralizedDynamicFactorModel common component over angular frequencies $|\theta| \in [\omega_{\text{low}}, \omega_{\text{high}}]$.

\[\chi_t^{[\omega_1, \omega_2]} = \frac{1}{\pi} \sum_{k=-M}^{M} K_k^{[\omega_1, \omega_2]} X_{t-k}\]

where:

  • $\chi_t^{[\omega_1, \omega_2]}$ is the two-sided band-limited common component at time $t$
  • $[\omega_1, \omega_2] \subseteq [0, \pi]$ is the angular frequency band
  • $K_k^{[\omega_1, \omega_2]}$ is the band-restricted dynamic PCA projector kernel at lag $k$
  • $M$ is the finite symmetric filter half-width
  • $X_{t-k}$ is the panel observation vector at time $t - k$

Fields

  • common_twosided::Matrix{T}: Two-sided band-limited common component (T_obs × N)
  • common_onesided::Matrix{T}: Real-time one-sided generalized-PC band projection (T_obs × N)
  • variance_share::Vector{T}: Share of each variable's common spectral variance in band (length N)
  • band::Tuple{T,T}: Angular frequency band (omega_low, omega_high) ⊆ [0, π]
  • r::Int: Static factor rank used for the one-sided projection
  • varnames::Vector{String}: Panel variable names (length N)
source
MacroEconometricModels.EurocoinResult — Type
EurocoinResult{T<:AbstractFloat}

Real-time low-frequency growth indicator constructed by projecting the medium-to-long-run component of target ($|\theta| \le \text{cutoff}$) onto the one-sided FHLR generalized static factor space $Z' X_t$ (Altissimo et al., 2010):

\[c_t = \bar{y} + w^{\top} (X_t - \bar{X}), \qquad w_{\text{fac}} = (Z^{\top} \hat{\Gamma}_X(0) Z)^{-1} \int_{-\omega_c}^{\omega_c} \hat{\Sigma}_{F,y}(\theta)\, d\theta\]

where:

  • $c_t$ is the real-time Eurocoin indicator at time $t$
  • $\bar{y}$ and $\bar{X}$ are sample means of target and X
  • $w$ and $w_{\text{fac}}$ are the observable and factor-space projection weight vectors
  • $X_t$ is the observed $N \times 1$ panel vector at time $t$
  • $Z$ is the $N \times r$ FHLR generalized principal component weight matrix
  • $F_t = Z^{\top} X_t$ are the contemporaneous static factors
  • $\hat{\Gamma}_X(0)$ is the lag-0 panel covariance reconstructed from the spectral density
  • $\omega_c$ is the low-pass angular frequency cutoff (cutoff)
  • $\hat{\Sigma}_{F,y}(\theta)$ is the estimated cross-spectral density between $F_t$ and centered target

Fields

  • indicator::Vector{T}: Strictly one-sided real-time growth indicator (T_obs)
  • target_lowpass::Vector{T}: Two-sided ideal low-pass filtered target benchmark (T_obs)
  • weights::Vector{T}: Linear projection weights mapping demeaned raw X to indicator (length N)
  • r2::T: In-sample $R^2$ of indicator against target_lowpass
  • cutoff::T: Angular frequency cutoff $\omega_c \in (0, \pi)$
  • q::Int: Number of dynamic common shocks
  • r::Int: Static factor dimension size(Z, 2)
source
MacroEconometricModels.SpectralFEVDResult — Type
SpectralFEVDResult{T<:AbstractFloat} <: AbstractFEVD

Frequency-band forecast error variance decomposition for a StructuralDFM over $|\omega| \in [\omega_{\text{low}}, \omega_{\text{high}}] \subseteq [0, \pi]$:

\[\text{FEVD}_{i,j}([\omega_1, \omega_2]) = \frac{\int_{\omega_1}^{\omega_2} |\Theta_{i,j}(\omega)|^2\, d\omega}{\sum_{s} \int_{\omega_1}^{\omega_2} S_{i,s}(\omega)\, d\omega}\]

where:

  • $\Theta_{i,j}(\omega)$ is the structural panel transfer function from shock $j$ to variable $i$
  • $S_{i,s}(\omega)$ is the band spectral power contributed by component $s$ (shocks and optionally idiosyncratic)
  • $\omega_1, \omega_2$ are the lower and upper angular frequency bounds

Fields

  • shares::Matrix{T}: Variance shares (N × q or N × (q + 1)), rows sum to 1
  • power_band::Matrix{T}: Band-integrated spectral variance contributions (N × n_shocks)
  • total_power_band::Vector{T}: Total band spectral power per panel variable (N)
  • band::Tuple{T,T}: Angular frequency band (omega_low, omega_high)
  • include_idiosyncratic::Bool: Whether an "Idiosyncratic" column is included
  • varnames::Vector{String}: Panel variable names (length N)
  • shock_names::Vector{String}: Shock (and optional idiosyncratic) labels
source
MacroEconometricModels.TimeVaryingGDFM — Type
TimeVaryingGDFM{T<:AbstractFloat} <: AbstractFactorModel

Locally stationary time-varying Generalized Dynamic Factor Model with smoothly varying time-frequency spectral representation in rescaled time $u = t / T$:

\[X_{t} = \chi_{t} + \xi_{t}, \qquad \Sigma_X(u, \theta) = L(u, \theta) \Lambda_q(u, \theta) L(u, \theta)^{H} + \Sigma_\xi(u, \theta)\]

where:

  • $X_{t}$ is the $N \times 1$ observed panel vector at time $t$
  • $\chi_{t}$ is the time-varying $q$-factor common component at time $t$
  • $\xi_{t}$ is the weakly cross-correlated idiosyncratic component at time $t$
  • $\Sigma_X(u, \theta)$ is the local evolutionary spectral density matrix at rescaled time $u \in (0, 1)$ and angular frequency $\theta \in [0, \pi]$
  • $L(u, \theta)$ is the $N \times q$ matrix of top-$q$ dynamic eigenvectors of $\Sigma_X(u, \theta)$
  • $\Lambda_q(u, \theta)$ is the $q \times q$ diagonal matrix of top-$q$ dynamic eigenvalues
  • $\Sigma_\xi(u, \theta)$ is the residual idiosyncratic local spectral density matrix

Fields

  • X::Matrix{T}: Original panel data (T_obs × N)
  • common_component::Matrix{T}: Reconstructed time-varying common component (T_obs × N)
  • idiosyncratic::Matrix{T}: Idiosyncratic remainder X - common_component (T_obs × N)
  • eigenvalues_path::Array{T,3}: Descending local spectral eigenvalues (n_grid × N × n_freq)
  • frequencies::Vector{T}: Angular frequency grid in [0, π] (length n_freq)
  • u_grid::Vector{T}: Rescaled time grid points in (0, 1) (length n_grid)
  • variance_share_path::Matrix{T}: Time-varying normalized top-q variance shares (T_obs × q)
  • q::Int: Number of dynamic common factors
  • n_grid::Int: Number of rescaled time grid points
  • time_bandwidth::T: Local time-kernel bandwidth b_T
  • lag_bandwidth::Int: Lag-window truncation M_T
  • time_kernel::Symbol: Time-smoothing kernel (:epanechnikov, :gaussian, or :uniform)
  • lag_kernel::Symbol: Spectral lag-window kernel (:bartlett, :parzen, or :tukey)
  • standardized::Bool: Whether estimation standardized X prior to filtering
  • varnames::Vector{String}: Panel variable names (length N)
source
MacroEconometricModels.GDFMNowcastResult — Type
GDFMNowcastResult{T<:AbstractFloat}

Result of ragged-edge Wiener–Kolmogorov nowcasting and missing-data imputation from an estimated GeneralizedDynamicFactorModel:

\[\hat{\chi}_{T_r + h \mid T_r} = C_{\chi, \text{obs}} \Sigma_{\text{obs}, \text{obs}}^{-1} X_{\text{obs}}, \qquad \text{MSE}_i = \left(\hat{\Gamma}_{\chi}(0) - C_{\chi, \text{obs}} \Sigma_{\text{obs}, \text{obs}}^{-1} C_{\text{obs}, \chi}\right)_{ii} + \max\left(\hat{\Gamma}_{\xi}(0)_{ii},\, 0\right)\]

where:

  • $\hat{\chi}_{T_r + h \mid T_r}$ is the conditional expectation of the $N \times 1$ common component at horizon $T_r + h$
  • $X_{\text{obs}}$ stacks all finite entries in the ragged-edge window $X_{\text{ragged}}[\max(1, T_r - \text{n\_lags}):T_r, :]$
  • $\Sigma_{\text{obs}, \text{obs}}$ is the spectral autocovariance matrix of $X_{\text{obs}}$
  • $C_{\chi, \text{obs}} = C_{\text{obs}, \chi}^{\top}$ is the cross-covariance between $\chi_{T_r + h}$ and $X_{\text{obs}}$
  • $\hat{\Gamma}_{\chi}(0)$ and $\hat{\Gamma}_{\xi}(0)$ are the lag-0 common and idiosyncratic covariance matrices
  • $\text{MSE}_i$ is the theoretical predictive mean squared error for variable $i$ prior to unstandardization

Fields

  • nowcast::Vector{T}: Unstandardized target common-component nowcast/forecast at $T_r + h$ (length N)
  • se::Vector{T}: Unstandardized conditional predictive standard errors (length N)
  • panel_imputed::Matrix{T}: Unstandardized panel (T_r × N) with missing entries imputed by row-wise conditional expectation and observed entries preserved
  • horizon::Int: Nowcast/forecast horizon h relative to T_r
  • n_lags::Int: Lag window depth n_lags used in conditioning
  • observed_mask::BitMatrix: Observation mask (T_r × N, true where X_ragged is finite)
  • varnames::Vector{String}: Variable names inherited from the GDFM (length N)
source

FHLZModel{T<:AbstractFloat} <: AbstractFactorModel

One-sided infinite-dimensional Generalized Dynamic Factor Model estimated via Forni, Hallin, Lippi & Zaffaroni (2015, 2017) random-permutation blockwise VARs.

Fields

  • X::Matrix{T}: Original panel data (T_obs × N)
  • q::Int: Number of dynamic common shocks
  • common_component::Matrix{T}: One-sided common component χ̂_t (T_eff × N, T_eff = T_obs - p_max)
  • idiosyncratic::Matrix{T}: Idiosyncratic component X[(p_max+1):end, :] - common_component (T_eff × N)
  • innovations::Matrix{T}: Orthonormal one-sided common shocks û_t (T_eff × q)
  • impulse_filters::Array{T,3}: One-sided MA filters B̂_0, …, B̂_{H-1} (H_irf × N × q)
  • p_blocks::Matrix{Int}: Selected block VAR lag orders (n_perm × n_blocks)
  • n_perm::Int: Number of cross-sectional permutations averaged
  • bandwidth::Int: Lag-window truncation parameter M
  • kernel::Symbol: Spectral lag-window kernel (:bartlett, :parzen, :tukey)
  • standardized::Bool: Whether estimation standardized X prior to filtering
  • variance_explained::Vector{T}: Share of spectral trace explained by each of the q dynamic factors
  • varnames::Vector{String}: Panel variable names (length N)

MacroEconometricModels.LPFEVD — Type
LPFEVD{T} <: AbstractFEVD

LP-based Forecast Error Variance Decomposition (Gorodnichenko & Lee 2019).

Uses R²-based estimator: regress estimated LP forecast errors on identified structural shocks to measure the share of forecast error variance attributable to each shock. Includes VAR-based bootstrap bias correction and CIs.

Fields

  • proportions: Raw FEVD estimates (n × n × H), [i,j,h] = share of variable i's h-step forecast error variance due to shock j
  • bias_corrected: Bias-corrected FEVD (n × n × H)
  • se: Bootstrap standard errors (n × n × H)
  • ci_lower: Lower CI bounds (n × n × H)
  • ci_upper: Upper CI bounds (n × n × H)
  • method: Estimator (:r2, :lpa, :lpb)
  • horizon: Maximum FEVD horizon
  • n_boot: Number of bootstrap replications used
  • conf_level: Confidence level for CIs
  • bias_correction: Whether bias correction was applied

Reference

Gorodnichenko, Y. & Lee, B. (2019). "Forecast Error Variance Decompositions with Local Projections." JBES, 38(4), 921–933.

MacroEconometricModels.StateLPModel — Type
StateLPModel{T} <: AbstractLPModel

State-dependent Local Projection (Auerbach & Gorodnichenko 2013).

Model: y{t+h} = F(zt)[αE + βE * shockt + ...] + (1-F(zt))[αR + βR * shock_t + ...]

F(z) is a smooth transition function, typically logistic. State E = expansion (high z), State R = recession (low z).

  • response_vars: Response variable indices

  • lags: Number of control lags

  • B_expansion: Coefficients in expansion state (per horizon)
  • B_recession: Coefficients in recession state (per horizon)
  • residuals: Residuals per horizon
  • vcov_expansion: Covariance in expansion (per horizon)
  • vcov_recession: Covariance in recession (per horizon)
  • vcov_diff: Covariance of difference (per horizon)
  • T_eff: Effective sample sizes
  • cov_estimator: Covariance estimator used

MacroEconometricModels.StateTransition — Type
StateTransition{T} <: Any

Smooth state transition function for state-dependent LP.

F(zt) = 1 / (1 + exp(-γ(zt - c))) (increasing in z: F → 1 for high/expansion states)

Fields:

  • state_var: State variable values (standardized)
  • gamma: Transition smoothness parameter (higher = sharper)
  • threshold: Transition threshold c
  • method: Transition function type (:logistic, :exponential, :indicator)
  • F_values: Precomputed transition function values

Prior Types

MacroEconometricModels.MinnesotaHyperparameters — Type
MinnesotaHyperparameters{T} <: AbstractPrior

Minnesota prior hyperparameters (Bańbura–Giannone–Reichlin stacked-dummy parameterization).

  • tau — overall shrinkage, as inverse tightness: dummy observations are divided by tau, so LARGER tau ⇒ LOOSER prior. (Opposite direction to the reference BVAR_'s mnprior.tight, which multiplies.)
  • decay — lag decay; higher lags shrink toward zero faster (scaling lag^decay).
  • lambda — weight on the sum-of-coefficients prior (shrinks toward Σₗ Aₗ = I).
  • mu — weight on the co-persistence / dummy-initial-observation prior.
  • omega — residual-covariance prior weight: the number of replications of the diag(σ̂) dummy block (rounded, minimum 1 when positive). Each copy adds n prior degrees of freedom around the same location E[Σ] ∝ diag(σ̂²), so LARGER omega ⇒ TIGHTER (more informative) residual-covariance prior — opposite direction to tau. omega ≤ 0 omits the covariance dummy block.

Reference-naming caveat (audit F-03): in Ferroni–Canova BVAR_/rfvar3, lambda is co-persistence and mu is own/sum-of-coefficients — i.e. our lambda/mu roles are SWAPPED relative to that toolbox. The defaults lambda=5, mu=2 reuse the reference's numeric guidance under our role assignment.

source
MacroEconometricModels.AbstractPrior — Type

Abstract supertype for Bayesian prior specifications.

  • prior::Symbol: Prior used (:normal or :minnesota)
  • sampler::Symbol: Sampler used (:direct or :gibbs)
  • varnames::Vector{String}: Variable names
  • manifest::Union{ReproManifest,Nothing}: Provenance record when estimate_bvar was called with seed=; reproduce(post) re-runs from that seed

TVPVARPosterior{T}

  • H_draws::Array{T,3} — n_draws × T_eff × n log-variances log σ²_{i,t}. This is the Kim-Shephard-Chib state convention (y* = log(e²) = h + log ε²), so the standard deviation is exp(h/2); volatility_path returns it directly
  • Q_draws::Array{T,3} — n_draws × k × k coefficient random-walk covariance
  • S_draws::Array{T,3} — n_draws × n_a × n_a block-diagonal A_t random-walk covariance
  • W_draws::Matrix{T} — n_draws × n log-volatility random-walk variances
  • Y::Matrix{T} — the original data

  • n_train::Int — training-sample length used to calibrate the priors

MacroEconometricModels.GLPHyperparameters — Type
GLPHyperparameters{T}

Result of the Giannone-Lenza-Primiceri hierarchical hyperparameter optimization.

  • log_ml::T — log marginal likelihood at the optimum
  • log_posterior::T — log_ml plus the log hyperprior density, the maximized objective
  • converged::Bool — the optimizer converged and no hyperparameter sits on a bound
  • at_bound::Bool — some hyperparameter is pinned to the edge of the search box
  • iterations::Int — optimizer iterations

A result with converged == false must not be treated as an estimate: that is exactly how a wildly out-of-range shrinkage value gets reported downstream as if it were selected.

Time-Varying Parameter VAR

MacroEconometricModels.estimate_tvpvar — Function
estimate_tvpvar(Y, p; kwargs...) -> TVPVARPosterior

Estimate a time-varying-parameter VAR with stochastic volatility (Primiceri 2005) by Gibbs sampling, or its constant-coefficient special case (Cogley & Sargent 2005) with tvp=false.

Model

\[y_t = X_t' B_t + A_t^{-1}\Sigma_t\varepsilon_t,\qquad B_t = B_{t-1} + \nu_t,\quad a_t = a_{t-1} + \zeta_t,\quad \log\sigma^2_{i,t} = \log\sigma^2_{i,t-1} + \eta_{i,t}\]

where:

  • $X_t' = I_n \otimes [1, y_{t-1}', \ldots, y_{t-p}']$ so $B_t$ stacks all equations
  • $A_t$ is lower triangular with a unit diagonal; its free elements are the structural contemporaneous coefficients
  • $\Sigma_t = \mathrm{diag}(\sigma_{1,t}, \ldots, \sigma_{n,t})$
  • $\nu_t \sim N(0,Q)$, $\zeta_t \sim N(0,S)$ (block diagonal by equation), $\eta_t \sim N(0,W)$ (diagonal)

Gibbs blocks

Each sweep draws, in the Del Negro & Primiceri (2015) corrected order:

  1. B_{1:T} by Carter-Kohn, with per-period observation covariance $A_t^{-1}\Sigma_t^2 A_t^{-1\prime}$
  2. a_{1:T} equation by equation, each a univariate Carter-Kohn on the VAR residuals
  3. the mixture indicators s_t given the current volatilities, then log σ²_{1:T} given s — the corrigendum's point is that s must be drawn before, not after, the volatility update, and that the coefficient blocks are drawn without conditioning on s
  4. Q, S, W from inverse-Wishart / inverse-gamma conjugate updates

Arguments

  • Y::AbstractMatrix — T × n data, time in rows
  • p::Int — lag order

Keywords

  • tvp::Bool=true — let B_t and a_t drift; false gives the Cogley-Sargent SV-BVAR
  • sv::Bool=true — let the volatilities drift; false fixes them at their training values
  • n_draws::Int=2000 — retained posterior draws
  • n_burn::Int=1000 — burn-in sweeps discarded
  • thin::Int=1 — keep every thin-th post-burn-in sweep
  • n_train::Int=0 — training-sample length for the prior; 0 uses max(4p+n+2, T÷4)
  • k_Q::Real=0.01, k_S::Real=0.1, k_W::Real=0.01 — Primiceri's prior-drift constants
  • varnames::Vector{String} — variable names
  • rng::AbstractRNG=Random.default_rng() — random number generator

Returns

TVPVARPosterior.

source
MacroEconometricModels.volatility_path — Function
volatility_path(post::TVPVARPosterior; quantile_levels=[0.16, 0.5, 0.84])
    -> (mean, quantiles)

Posterior mean and credible bands of the standard-deviation path σ_{i,t} = exp(h_{i,t}/2), where h is the stored log-variance state. Returns a T_eff × n mean matrix and a T_eff × n × n_q quantile array.

source
MacroEconometricModels.irf — Method
irf(post::TVPVARPosterior, horizon; t=post.T_eff, n_draws=500, quantile_levels=..., rng=...)
    -> BayesianImpulseResponse

Impulse response at date t of the effective sample, integrating over posterior draws.

At each draw the coefficients B_t give the VAR companion form and the structural impact matrix is A_t^{-1}Σ_t — the same recursive identification Primiceri's A_t encodes, but with both the propagation and the impact matrix taken at date t. Comparing irf(post, H; t=t₁) with irf(post, H; t=t₂) is how time variation in the transmission mechanism is read off.

Draws whose companion matrix is explosive are dropped and counted (reported on the result as n_failed), matching the BVAR IRF convention.

Keywords

  • t::Int=post.T_eff — date within the effective sample (1 = first post-training period)
  • n_draws::Int=500 — posterior draws used (capped at the number available)
  • quantile_levels::Vector{<:Real}=DEFAULT_QUANTILES_MACRO — credible-band levels (default [0.16, 0.5, 0.84], 68%)
  • stationary_only::Bool=true — drop explosive draws
source

Mixed-Frequency VAR

MacroEconometricModels.estimate_mfvar — Function
estimate_mfvar(data, p; low_freq=Int[], freq_ratio=3, aggregation=:growth, kwargs...)
    -> MFVARPosterior

Mixed-frequency Bayesian VAR (Schorfheide & Song 2015).

data is a high-frequency T × n panel with NaN wherever a series is not observed: a quarterly series in a monthly panel carries a value every third row and NaN elsewhere. The VAR itself lives entirely at the high frequency; the low-frequency observations are temporal aggregates of the latent high-frequency path,

\[y^{lo}_{i,t} = \sum_{j} w_j\, z_{i,t-j+1}\]

with weights set by aggregation (see _mf_agg_weights for the exact rules; :growth uses the Mariano-Murasawa triangular filter).

A two-block Gibbs sampler alternates between the conjugate NIW draw of (B, Σ) given the completed path and a Durbin-Koopman simulation-smoother draw of the path given (B, Σ). With low_freq empty the first block is the only source of randomness in the parameters and the sampler reduces to the conjugate BVAR.

Arguments

  • data::AbstractMatrix — T × n high-frequency panel, NaN where unobserved
  • p::Int — lag order at the high frequency

Keywords

  • low_freq::Vector{Int}=Int[] — column indices observed at the low frequency
  • freq_ratio::Int=3 — high-frequency periods per low-frequency period (3 = monthly/quarterly)
  • aggregation::Union{Symbol,Vector{Symbol}}=:growth — :growth, :flow, :average or :stock, either one rule for all low-frequency series or one per series
  • n_draws::Int=1000 — retained posterior draws
  • n_burn::Int=500 — burn-in sweeps
  • prior::Symbol=:minnesota — :minnesota (dummy observations) or :diffuse
  • hyper::Union{Nothing,MinnesotaHyperparameters}=nothing — fixed Minnesota hyperparameters; nothing optimizes them once on the initial path
  • varnames::Vector{String} — variable names
  • rng::AbstractRNG=Random.default_rng() — random number generator

Returns

MFVARPosterior.

References

  • Schorfheide, F. & Song, D. (2015). Journal of Business & Economic Statistics, 33(3), 366-380.
source
MacroEconometricModels.latent_path — Function
latent_path(post::MFVARPosterior; quantile_levels=[0.16, 0.5, 0.84]) -> (mean, quantiles)

Posterior mean and credible bands of the latent high-frequency paths. Returns a T_hf × n mean matrix and a T_hf × n × n_q quantile array. For high-frequency series the path reproduces the data exactly, so its bands are degenerate.

source

Hyperparameter Selection

MacroEconometricModels.optimize_hyperparameters_glp — Function
optimize_hyperparameters_glp(Y, p; kwargs...) -> GLPHyperparameters

Joint Giannone-Lenza-Primiceri (2015) hyperparameter selection: maximize the log marginal likelihood of the conjugate Minnesota BVAR plus GLP's Gamma hyperpriors, over the overall tightness tau, the sum-of-coefficients tightness lambda, and the dummy-initial- observation tightness mu, jointly.

\[\hat\gamma = \arg\max_\gamma\ \log p(Y\mid\gamma) + \log p(\gamma)\]

where:

  • $p(Y\mid\gamma)$ is the closed-form conjugate marginal likelihood of the dummy-observation representation (log_marginal_likelihood)
  • $p(\gamma)$ are Gamma hyperpriors in GLP's mode/standard-deviation parameterization: tau mode 0.2 sd 0.4, lambda mode 1 sd 1, mu mode 1 sd 1

This differs from optimize_hyperparameters, which grid-searches tau alone and leaves the others at their defaults — a search that cannot trade overall tightness against the sum-of-coefficients and initial-observation priors, and so can drive tau to an extreme value that is then reported as if it had been selected.

Optimization runs in log space (keeping every hyperparameter positive without a constrained solver) with a derivative-free Nelder-Mead, restarted from several dispersed starting points because the marginal-likelihood surface is not concave in the hyperparameters. converged is true only when the optimizer converged and no hyperparameter sits on a bound.

Keywords

  • decay::Real=0.5 / omega::Real=1.0 — held fixed (GLP fix the lag decay rather than estimate it); pass different values to shift them
  • starts::Int=4 — dispersed restarts of the optimizer
  • max_iter::Int=500 — iterations per restart
  • f_reltol::Real=1e-8 — relative objective tolerance
  • verbose::Bool=true — warn on non-convergence

Returns

GLPHyperparameters.

References

  • Giannone, D., Lenza, M. & Primiceri, G. E. (2015). Review of Economics and Statistics, 97(2), 436-451.
source

MacroEconometricModels.VARForecast — Type

source
MacroEconometricModels.ForecastCondition — Type
ForecastCondition{T}

One conditioning restriction: variable takes value at forecast horizon.

sd == 0 is a hard condition (the path is hit exactly). sd > 0 is a soft condition — the restriction is treated as a noisy observation with standard deviation sd, so the conditional shock distribution shrinks toward the target instead of pinning it, and sd → 0 recovers the hard case.

Build with forecast_condition.

source

Conditional Forecasting

MacroEconometricModels.conditional_forecast — Function
conditional_forecast(model::VARModel, conditions, h; kwargs...) -> ConditionalForecast

Waggoner-Zha (1999) conditional forecast: project the VAR h periods ahead subject to conditions on the paths of selected variables.

Write the forecast as the unconditional path plus the structural moving average of the future shocks,

\[y_{T+s} = \hat y_{T+s} + \sum_{j=1}^{s} \Psi_{s-j}\, \varepsilon_{T+j}, \qquad \Psi_s = \Phi_s P,\]

where $P$ is the structural impact matrix. Each condition is linear in $\varepsilon$, so stacking them gives $R\varepsilon = r$ and the shocks are drawn from the implied conditional distribution

\[\varepsilon \sim N\big(R'(RR')^{-1}r,\; I - R'(RR')^{-1}R\big),\]

the minimum-norm mean plus randomness in the null space of the restrictions. The point forecast uses the conditional mean; the bands come from reps draws.

Identification and conditional forecasts

For conditions on observable paths, the conditional forecast is invariant to the rotation $Q$: replacing $P = L Q$ by $L$ leaves $R'(RR')^{-1}r$ mapped through the moving average unchanged, because $Q$ cancels between the restriction matrix and the impact matrix. Identification therefore does not change the forecast; it changes only the interpretation of the implied shocks in result.shocks, which rotate as $\varepsilon_L = Q\,\varepsilon_{LQ}$. Pass Q when the implied structural shocks are themselves of interest, or as the basis for restrictions on shocks rather than observables (structural scenario analysis, Antolín-Díaz, Petrella & Rubio-Ramírez 2021). The default is a Cholesky factorization in variable order.

Arguments

  • model::VARModel{T} — estimated VAR
  • conditions — a Vector{ForecastCondition} (see forecast_condition) or a Dict keyed by (variable, horizon) with values value or (value, sd)
  • h::Int — forecast horizon

Keywords

  • Q::Union{Nothing,AbstractMatrix}=nothing — rotation matrix; nothing = Cholesky. Only affects the reported shocks (the forecast itself is rotation-invariant, see above)
  • reps::Int=1000 — draws used for the bands
  • conf_level::Real=0.68 — band coverage (DEFAULT_CI_MACRO)
  • rng::AbstractRNG=Random.default_rng() — random number generator

Returns

ConditionalForecast.

References

  • Waggoner, D. F. & Zha, T. (1999). Conditional Forecasts in Dynamic Multivariate Models. Review of Economics and Statistics, 81(4), 639-651.
source
conditional_forecast(post::BVARPosterior, conditions, h; kwargs...) -> ConditionalForecast

Waggoner-Zha conditional forecast integrating over the BVAR posterior. Each posterior draw supplies its own coefficients, structural impact matrix, unconditional path and restriction system, and contributes one conditional shock draw — so the bands reflect both parameter and shock uncertainty, unlike the VARModel method which conditions on the point estimate.

Non-stationary posterior draws are skipped (as in forecast(::BVARPosterior, h)).

Keywords

  • Q::Union{Nothing,AbstractMatrix}=nothing — rotation matrix; nothing = Cholesky. Only affects the reported shocks (the forecast itself is rotation-invariant, see above)
  • reps::Union{Nothing,Int}=nothing — posterior draws used (default: all)
  • conf_level::Real=0.68 — credible-band coverage (DEFAULT_CI_MACRO)
  • point_estimate::Symbol=:mean — :mean or :median across draws
  • rng::AbstractRNG=Random.default_rng() — random number generator
source
MacroEconometricModels.forecast_condition — Function
forecast_condition(variable, horizon, value; sd=0.0) -> ForecastCondition

Build one conditioning restriction for conditional_forecast.

variable is a column index, name String, or Symbol. sd=0 (the default) makes the condition hard; a positive sd makes it soft (a target with tolerance).

conds = [forecast_condition("INFL", 1, 2.0),
         forecast_condition("INFL", 2, 2.0; sd=0.25)]

FAVAR Models

MacroEconometricModels.FAVARModel — Type
FAVARModel{T} <: AbstractVARModel

Factor-Augmented VAR model (Bernanke, Boivin & Eliasz, 2005).

The FAVAR system estimates a VAR on [Ft, Ykey,t] where Ft are latent factors extracted from a large panel X, and Ykey are observed key variables (e.g., the federal funds rate).

Fields

  • Y::Matrix{T}: Augmented data [F, Ykey] used in VAR (Teff × (r+n_key))
  • p::Int: VAR lag order
  • B::Matrix{T}: VAR coefficient matrix ((1+p*(r+nkey)) × (r+nkey))
  • U::Matrix{T}: VAR residuals
  • Sigma::Matrix{T}: Residual covariance
  • varnames::Vector{String}: Variable names in VAR (["F1","F2",...,"Y1","Y2",...])
  • X_panel::Matrix{T}: Original panel data (T_obs × N)
  • panel_varnames::Vector{String}: Panel variable names (length N)
  • Y_key_indices::Vector{Int}: Column indices of key variables in X_panel
  • n_factors::Int: Number of latent factors r
  • n_key::Int: Number of key observed variables
  • factors::Matrix{T}: Extracted factors (T_obs × r)
  • loadings::Matrix{T}: Factor loadings (N × r)
  • factor_model::FactorModel{T}: Underlying factor model from PCA
  • aic::T: Akaike information criterion
  • bic::T: Bayesian information criterion
  • loglik::T: Log-likelihood

MacroEconometricModels.BayesianFAVAR — Type
BayesianFAVAR{T}

Bayesian FAVAR with posterior draws of coefficients, covariances, factors, and loadings.

Fields

  • B_draws::Array{T,3}: Coefficient draws (ndraws × k × nvar)
  • Sigma_draws::Array{T,3}: Covariance draws (ndraws × nvar × n_var)
  • factor_draws::Array{T,3}: Factor draws (ndraws × Tobs × r)
  • loadings_draws::Array{T,3}: Loading draws (n_draws × N × r)
  • lambda_y_draws::Array{T,3}: Direct key-variable loading draws (ndraws × N × nkey), rescaled so that Λ_y · y_irf with y_irf in the VAR's original Y_key units yields a response in standardized panel units — the same units as Λ · f_irf
  • X_panel::Matrix{T}: Original panel data (T_obs × N)
  • panel_varnames::Vector{String}: Panel variable names
  • Y_key_indices::Vector{Int}: Key variable column indices
  • n_factors::Int: Number of factors
  • n_key::Int: Number of key variables
  • n::Int: Total VAR variables (r + n_key)
  • p::Int: VAR lag order
  • data::Matrix{T}: Augmented VAR data [F, Y_key]
  • varnames::Vector{String}: VAR variable names

Since the #528 fix, the Gibbs measurement equation is BBE (2005) eq. 3, X = F Λ' + Y_key Λ_y' + e, with the factors purged of the key variables' own variation. The #525 double-counting concern applied to the OLD equation without Λ_y (factors then absorbed the entire Y_key co-movement); with the factors purged, panel mappings MUST add the stored direct channel Λ_y · y_irf back, or responses of Y_key-loading series are understated. favar_panel_irf does this.

MacroEconometricModels.QuantileFAVAR — Type
QuantileFAVAR{T<:AbstractFloat}

Two-step Quantile Factor-Augmented VAR model combining quantile factor extraction (estimate_qfm), BBE (#528) purging of observed key variables Y_key, quantile measurement regressions, and a recursive structural QuantileVARModel on $[\tilde{F}_t(\tau), Y_{\text{key}, t}]$:

\[Q_\tau(X_{it} \mid \tilde{F}_t(\tau), Y_{\text{key}, t}) = c_i(\tau) + \lambda_i(\tau)' \tilde{F}_t(\tau) + \lambda_{y, i}(\tau)' Y_{\text{key}, t}\]

where:

  • $X_{it}$ is panel series $i$ at time $t$
  • $\tilde{F}_t(\tau)$ is the $r \times 1$ vector of slow-moving quantile factors purged of $Y_{\text{key}, t}$
  • $Y_{\text{key}, t}$ is the $n_{\text{key}} \times 1$ vector of observed key variables
  • $\lambda_i(\tau)$ and $\lambda_{y, i}(\tau)$ are the factor and direct key-variable quantile loadings

Fields

  • qvar::QuantileVARModel{T} — estimated QVAR on $[\tilde{F}_t(\tau), Y_{\text{key}, t}]$
  • factors_list::Vector{Matrix{T}} — per-quantile purged factor matrices ($T_{\text{obs}} \times r$)
  • loadings_list::Vector{Matrix{T}} — per-quantile factor loading matrices ($N \times r$)
  • lambda_y_list::Vector{Matrix{T}} — per-quantile direct key-variable loading matrices ($N \times n_{\text{key}}$)
  • intercept_list::Vector{Vector{T}} — per-quantile measurement intercepts (length $N$)
  • qfm_models::Vector{QuantileFactorModel{T}} — underlying quantile factor models per $\tau$
  • taus::Vector{T} — quantile levels strictly in $(0, 1)$
  • X_panel::Matrix{T} — original panel matrix ($T_{\text{obs}} \times N$)
  • Y_key::Matrix{T} — observed key variables ($T_{\text{obs}} \times n_{\text{key}}$)
  • Y_key_indices::Vector{Int} — column indices of key variables in X_panel (empty if external)
  • n_factors::Int — number of latent quantile factors $r$
  • n_key::Int — number of key observed variables $n_{\text{key}}$
  • p::Int — QVAR lag order
  • standardize::Bool — whether panel series were standardized during factor/measurement estimation
  • panel_varnames::Vector{String} — panel variable names (length $N$)
  • varnames::Vector{String} — augmented VAR variable names (length $r + n_{\text{key}}$)
  • manifest::Union{ReproManifest,Nothing} — reproducibility metadata
source
MacroEconometricModels.VBQuantileFAVAR — Type
VBQuantileFAVAR{T<:AbstractFloat}

Variational Bayes Quantile Factor-Augmented VAR model (Korobilis & Schröder 2025) using the Asymmetric Laplace normal-exponential mixture representation:

\[e_{it}(\tau) = \theta_\tau z_{it} + \sqrt{\kappa_\tau^2 \sigma_i z_{it}} \, \xi_{it}, \qquad \theta_\tau = \frac{1 - 2\tau}{\tau(1 - \tau)}, \qquad \kappa_\tau^2 = \frac{2}{\tau(1 - \tau)}\]

where:

  • $e_{it}(\tau)$ is the Asymmetric Laplace disturbance at quantile $\tau \in (0, 1)$
  • $z_{it} \sim \mathrm{Exp}(\sigma_i^{-1})$ is the latent exponential mixing variable
  • $\sigma_i$ is the equation scale parameter
  • $\xi_{it} \sim \mathcal{N}(0, 1)$ is a standard normal innovation

Fields

  • qvar::QuantileVARModel{T} — posterior-mean recursive structural QVAR across quantiles
  • factors_mean::Vector{Matrix{T}} — per-quantile variational posterior mean factors ($T_{\text{obs}} \times r$)
  • loadings_mean::Vector{Matrix{T}} — per-quantile posterior mean factor loadings ($N \times r$)
  • lambda_y_mean::Vector{Matrix{T}} — per-quantile posterior mean direct $Y_{\text{key}}$ loadings ($N \times n_{\text{key}}$)
  • intercept_mean::Vector{Vector{T}} — per-quantile posterior mean measurement intercepts (length $N$)
  • factor_cov::Vector{Array{T,3}} — per-quantile variational factor covariance tensors ($T_{\text{obs}} \times r \times r$)
  • var_cov::Vector{Vector{Matrix{T}}} — per-quantile, per-equation variational VAR coefficient covariance matrices
  • A0_draws::Vector{Array{T,3}} — per-quantile posterior draws of $A_0(\tau)$ ($n_{\text{draws}} \times n_{\text{aug}} \times n_{\text{aug}}$)
  • B_draws::Vector{Array{T,3}} — per-quantile posterior draws of $B(\tau)$ ($n_{\text{draws}} \times (1 + n_{\text{aug}} p) \times n_{\text{aug}}$)
  • loadings_draws::Vector{Array{T,3}} — per-quantile posterior draws of $\Lambda(\tau)$ ($n_{\text{draws}} \times N \times r$)
  • lambda_y_draws::Vector{Array{T,3}} — per-quantile posterior draws of $\Lambda_y(\tau)$ ($n_{\text{draws}} \times N \times n_{\text{key}}$)
  • taus::Vector{T} — quantile levels strictly in $(0, 1)$
  • X_panel::Matrix{T} — original panel matrix ($T_{\text{obs}} \times N$)
  • Y_key::Matrix{T} — observed key variables ($T_{\text{obs}} \times n_{\text{key}}$)
  • Y_key_indices::Vector{Int} — column indices of key variables in X_panel
  • n_factors::Int — number of latent factors $r$
  • n_key::Int — number of key observed variables $n_{\text{key}}$
  • p::Int — QVAR lag order
  • standardize::Bool — whether panel series were standardized during estimation
  • vb_converged::Vector{Bool} — CAVI convergence status per quantile
  • vb_iterations::Vector{Int} — CAVI iteration count per quantile
  • panel_varnames::Vector{String} — panel variable names (length $N$)
  • varnames::Vector{String} — augmented VAR variable names (length $r + n_{\text{key}}$)
  • manifest::Union{ReproManifest,Nothing} — reproducibility metadata

estimate_var(Y::AbstractMatrix{T}, p::Int; check_stability::Bool=true) -> VARModel{T}

MacroEconometricModels.forecast — Method
forecast(model::VARModel, h; ci_method=:bootstrap, reps=500, conf_level=DEFAULT_CI_MACRO,
         stationary_only=false, rng=Random.default_rng()) -> VARForecast{T}

Forecast from VAR model for h steps ahead with optional confidence intervals.

The point forecast iterates the VAR recursion forward using the estimated coefficients and the last p observations. Confidence bands are formed by one of:

  • :bootstrap (default) — Kilian (1998) bootstrap-B. Each replication draws a pseudo-sample Y* by resampling the residuals and iterating the VAR with the point estimate, re-estimates the coefficients on Y*, and then simulates the future path from the true last-p observations using the re-estimated coefficients plus fresh resampled future shocks. This propagates both future innovation uncertainty and coefficient-estimation uncertainty, so the bands are wider (and closer to nominal coverage in small samples) than a residual-only bootstrap. Cost: reps VAR re-estimations.
  • :analytic — Lütkepohl (2005, §3.5) known-coefficient forecast MSE Σ_y(h) = Σ_{i=0}^{h-1} Φ_i Σ_u Φ_i' with symmetric Gaussian bands point ± z·√diag. This ignores coefficient-estimation uncertainty (the leading asymptotic term); use :bootstrap for finite-sample parameter uncertainty.
  • :none — point forecast only (zero-width bands).

Arguments

  • model: Estimated VAR model
  • h: Forecast horizon (number of steps ahead)
  • ci_method: :bootstrap (default), :analytic, or :none
  • reps: Number of bootstrap replications (default 500)
  • conf_level: Confidence level for intervals (default 0.68, DEFAULT_CI_MACRO)
  • stationary_only: reject bootstrap draws whose re-estimated companion matrix is non-stationary (default false)
  • rng: random number generator for the bootstrap (default Random.default_rng())

Returns

VARForecast{T} with point forecasts and CIs.

Example

model = estimate_var(Y, 4)
fc = forecast(model, 12)  # 12-step ahead forecast with bootstrap-B CIs
source
MacroEconometricModels.select_lag_order — Method

Select optimal lag order via information criterion (:aic, :bic, :hqic).

All candidates are estimated on the common sample t = max_p+1, …, T (Lütkepohl 2005 §4.3; Stata varsoc): each candidate p drops the same max_p initial conditions, so the log det Σ̃ terms are computed on identical data and the criteria are comparable across p. Ragged per-candidate samples bias the selection toward max_p on short samples (#522).

source

Bayesian Estimation

Posterior sampling, posterior summary models, and predictive simulation for the BVAR.

MacroEconometricModels.estimate_bvar — Method
estimate_bvar(Y, p; n_draws=1000, sampler=:direct, burnin=0, thin=1,
              prior=:normal, hyper=nothing) -> BVARPosterior

Estimate Bayesian VAR via conjugate Normal-Inverse-Wishart posterior.

Model

Y = X B + E,    E ~ MN(0, Σ, I_T)
Prior: Σ ~ IW(ν₀, S₀),  vec(B)|Σ ~ N(b₀, Σ ⊗ V₀)

Samplers

  • :direct (default) — i.i.d. draws from analytical posterior. burnin and thin are ignored.
  • :gibbs — Standard two-block Gibbs sampler. burnin defaults to 200 if not specified.

Arguments

  • Y::AbstractMatrix: T × n data matrix
  • p::Int: Number of lags

Keyword Arguments

  • n_draws::Int=1000: Number of posterior draws to keep
  • sampler::Symbol=:direct: Sampling algorithm (:direct or :gibbs)
  • burnin::Int=0: Burnin period (only for :gibbs; defaults to 200 when sampler=:gibbs and burnin=0)
  • thin::Int=1: Thinning interval (only for :gibbs)
  • prior::Symbol=:normal: Prior type (:normal or :minnesota)
  • hyper::Union{Nothing,MinnesotaHyperparameters}=nothing: Minnesota hyperparameters. When prior=:minnesota and hyper=nothing, they are selected automatically (see hyperopt). Pass an explicit MinnesotaHyperparameters(...) to use fixed values instead.
  • hyperopt::Symbol=:glp: how hyperparameters are selected when hyper=nothing. :glp runs the full Giannone, Lenza & Primiceri (2015) joint optimization of the marginal likelihood plus Gamma hyperpriors over the overall, sum-of-coefficients and dummy-initial-observation tightness (optimize_hyperparameters_glp); :grid restores the historical tau-only grid (optimize_hyperparameters), which leaves the other hyperparameters at their defaults.

Returns

BVARPosterior{T} containing coefficient and covariance draws.

Example

Y = randn(200, 3)
post = estimate_bvar(Y, 2; n_draws=1000)
post_mn = estimate_bvar(Y, 2; prior=:minnesota, n_draws=500)
source
MacroEconometricModels.forecast — Method
forecast(post::BVARPosterior, h; reps=nothing, conf_level=DEFAULT_CI_MACRO, point_estimate=:mean,
         store_draws=false) -> BVARForecast{T}

Forecast from Bayesian VAR using posterior draws.

For each posterior draw, iterates the VAR recursion forward h steps. Returns posterior mean forecast with credible intervals from the distribution of forecast paths across draws.

Arguments

  • post: BVAR posterior
  • h: Forecast horizon

Keyword Arguments

  • reps: Number of posterior draws to use (default: all)
  • conf_level: Credible interval level (default: 0.68, DEFAULT_CI_MACRO)
  • point_estimate: :mean (default) or :median for central tendency
  • store_draws: retain the raw posterior forecast draws in _draws (nvalid × h × n; default false — quantiles only). Consumed by `policyforecast` (CF-05/#385).

Returns

BVARForecast{T} with posterior mean forecast and credible intervals.

Example

post = estimate_bvar(Y, 4; n_draws=1000)
fc = forecast(post, 12)

MacroEconometricModels.reproduce — Method
reproduce(post::BVARPosterior) -> ReproReport

Re-draw the posterior from the manifest's recorded seed and settings and check it matches the stored B_draws/Sigma_draws bit-for-bit. Self-contained: the posterior stores its data, lag order, prior, and sampler. Requires the posterior to have been estimated with estimate_bvar(Y, p; seed=N); without a recorded seed the report's matched is missing.

post = estimate_bvar(Y, 2; n_draws=500, seed=20260717)
reproduce(post)   # ReproReport: PASS

Prior Specification

MacroEconometricModels.gen_dummy_obs — Method
gen_dummy_obs(Y, p, hyper) -> (Y_dummy, X_dummy)

Generate Minnesota prior dummy observations (stacked-dummy / BGR parameterization). Hyperparameters (see MinnesotaHyperparameters for exact conventions): tau (inverse-tightness — larger ⇒ looser), decay, lambda (sum-of-coefficients), mu (co-persistence), omega (residual-covariance prior weight — the number of replications of the diag(σ̂) dummy block; larger ⇒ more prior degrees of freedom ⇒ tighter around the same location E[Σ] ∝ diag(σ̂²)). Note lambda/mu roles are swapped relative to the reference BVAR_/rfvar3 toolbox (audit F-03).

VECM Estimation

estimate_vecm supports automatic rank selection via the Johansen trace test (rank=:auto) or explicit rank specification, and all five Johansen (1995) deterministic specifications via integer IDs (1:5) or symbols:

  • Case 1: 1 or :none (no deterministic terms)
  • Case 2: 2, :constant, or :restricted_constant (restricted intercept in $\beta$; default)
  • Case 3: 3, :unrestricted_constant, or :drift (unrestricted constant in VAR)
  • Case 4: 4, :trend, or :restricted_trend (restricted linear trend in $\beta$)
  • Case 5: 5 or :unrestricted_trend (unrestricted trend in VAR)
MacroEconometricModels.estimate_vecm — Function
estimate_vecm(Y, p; rank=:auto, deterministic=:constant, method=:johansen, significance=0.05)

Estimate a Vector Error Correction Model.

Arguments

  • Y: Data matrix (T × n) in levels
  • p: Underlying VAR order (VECM has p-1 lagged differences)
  • rank: Cointegrating rank; :auto (default) selects via Johansen trace test, or specify an integer
  • deterministic: :none, :constant (default), or :trend
  • method: :johansen (default) or :engle_granger (bivariate, rank=1 only)
  • significance: Significance level for automatic rank selection (default 0.05)

Returns

VECMModel with estimated α, β, Γ matrices, residuals, and diagnostics.

Example

Y = cumsum(randn(200, 3), dims=1)
Y[:, 2] = Y[:, 1] + 0.1 * randn(200)
m = estimate_vecm(Y, 2)
source
MacroEconometricModels.to_var — Function
to_var(vecm::VECMModel) -> VARModel

Convert VECM to VAR in levels representation.

The VECM: ΔYₜ = ΠYₜ₋₁ + Σᵢ ΓᵢΔYₜ₋ᵢ + μ + uₜ maps to VAR: Yₜ = c + A₁Yₜ₋₁ + ... + AₚYₜ₋ₚ + uₜ

with:

  • A₁ = Π + Iₙ + Γ₁
  • Aᵢ = Γᵢ - Γᵢ₋₁ for i = 2, ..., p-1
  • Aₚ = -Γₚ₋₁
source
to_var(favar::FAVARModel) -> VARModel

Convert a FAVAR to a standard VARModel for use with irf, fevd, historical_decomposition, and other structural analysis methods.

This follows the same delegation pattern as to_var(vecm::VECMModel).

source

MacroEconometricModels.granger_causality_vecm — Function
granger_causality_vecm(vecm, cause, effect) -> VECMGrangerResult

Test Granger causality from variable cause to variable effect in a VECM.

Three tests are computed:

  1. Short-run: Wald test on Γ coefficients of the cause variable in the effect equation
  2. Long-run: Wald test on α coefficients in the effect equation (error correction channel)
  3. Strong: Joint test of both short-run and long-run

Arguments

  • vecm: Estimated VECMModel
  • cause: Index of the causing variable
  • effect: Index of the effect variable

Returns

VECMGrangerResult with test statistics, p-values, and degrees of freedom.

source
