MacroEconometricModels.irf — Method
irf(vecm::VECMModel, horizon; method=:cholesky, kwargs...) -> ImpulseResponse

Compute IRFs for a VECM. Cholesky, sign, narrative, and statistical methods convert to the levels VAR. method=:svec and method=:long_run use identify_svec; levels IRFs of permanent shocks converge to the corresponding column of $Ξ B_0$. ci_type=:bootstrap and ci_type=:theoretical throw ArgumentError on those methods: the levels-VAR resampler would inject a frozen SVEC rotation into every draw.

source
MacroEconometricModels.test_alpha_restriction — Method
test_alpha_restriction(m::VECMModel, A) -> VECMRestrictionTest

Johansen LR test that all adjustment vectors lie in a known space, α = Aψ, where A is p × a (a ≥ r). Solved on the dual reduced-rank device |λ A′S₀₀A − A′S₀₁ S₁₁⁻¹ S₁₀A| = 0; LR = T Σ_{i=1}^r ln[(1−λ*_i)/(1−λ_i)] ∼ χ²(df) with df = r(p − a).

The restricted_model keeps the (superconsistent) unrestricted β and projects α onto span(A).

source
MacroEconometricModels.test_beta_restriction — Method
test_beta_restriction(m::VECMModel, H) -> VECMRestrictionTest

Johansen LR test that every cointegrating vector lies in a known space, β = Hφ, where H is p × s (p = nvars(m), s ≥ r = cointegrating_rank(m)).

The restricted eigenvalues λ*_i solve the transformed eigenproblem |λ H′S₁₁H − H′S₁₀ S₀₀⁻¹ S₀₁H| = 0; the statistic is LR = T Σ_{i=1}^r ln[(1−λ*_i)/(1−λ_i)] ∼ χ²(df) with df = r(p − s). The non-binding case H = Iₚ (s = p) returns LR ≈ 0, df = 0.

The returned test carries a re-estimated restricted_model whose β lies in span(H), ready for irf/fevd/historical_decomposition.

source
MacroEconometricModels.test_joint_restriction — Method
test_joint_restriction(m::VECMModel, H, A; maxiter=1000, tol=1e-8) -> VECMRestrictionTest

Joint LR test of β = Hφ and α = Aψ, estimated by the Johansen–Juselius switching algorithm (alternate: β given α, then α given β, until the restricted log-likelihood change < tol). The statistic compares the restricted and unrestricted Gaussian log-likelihoods, LR = T (ln|Ω̂_r| − ln|Ω̂_u|) ∼ χ²(df) with df = r(p − s) + r(p − a). Non-convergence within maxiter sets converged = false and emits a warning.

source
MacroEconometricModels.test_known_beta — Method
test_known_beta(m::VECMModel, b) -> VECMRestrictionTest

Johansen LR test of a fully specified cointegrating space β = b, where b is p × r. Uses the transformed eigenproblem with H = b (degenerate s = r case); df = r(p − r). Setting b = m.beta (the estimated β) returns LR ≈ 0.

source
MacroEconometricModels.test_weak_exogeneity — Method
test_weak_exogeneity(m::VECMModel, vars) -> VECMRestrictionTest

Test weak exogeneity of the named vars (indices, names, or a vector thereof) for the long-run parameters — the headline central-bank question, e.g. is the policy rate weakly exogenous for the cointegrating relations? It is the leading α-restriction special case in which the α rows of vars are zero; A selects the complementary (error-correcting) rows. With m = length(vars), df = r·m.

source
MacroEconometricModels.forecast — Method
forecast(vecm::VECMModel, h; ci_method=:none, reps=500, conf_level=DEFAULT_CI_MACRO) -> VECMForecast

Forecast from a VECM by iterating the VECM equations in levels.

Unlike VAR forecasting, this preserves the cointegrating relationships in the forecast path.

Arguments

  • vecm: Estimated VECM
  • h: Forecast horizon
  • ci_method: :none (default), :bootstrap, or :simulation
  • reps: Number of bootstrap/simulation replications (default 500)
  • conf_level: Confidence level (default 0.68, DEFAULT_CI_MACRO)

Returns

VECMForecast with level and difference forecasts, plus CIs if requested.

MacroEconometricModels.irf — Method
irf(model, horizon; method=:cholesky, ci_type=:none, reps=200, conf_level=DEFAULT_CI_MACRO, ...)

Compute IRFs with optional confidence intervals.

Methods

:cholesky, :sign, :narrative, :long_run, :proxy, :ab, :max_share, :svec, :fastica, :jade, :sobi, :dcov, :hsic, :student_t, :mixture_normal, :pml, :skew_normal, :nongaussian_ml, :markov_switching, :garch, :smooth_transition, :external_volatility

Note: :smooth_transition requires transition_var kwarg. :external_volatility requires regime_indicator kwarg. :proxy requires instruments (length T or T_eff) and is partial when k < n. Default normalize=:unit_effect. With ci_type=:bootstrap, jointly resamples (u_t, z_t) and rewrites bootstrap=:iid to :block (Jentsch & Lunsford 2019); the reproducibility manifest records :block. :ab requires pattern::SVARPattern (Amisano–Giannini AB-model ML). :max_share requires target (variable index or name) and is partial; pass horizons (time domain, default 0:20) or band=(ω₁, ω₂). :svec is the structural-VECM route (identify_svec); call it on a VECMModel via irf(vecm; method=:svec) or method=:long_run. normalize=:unit_variance (default) leaves the identified rotation unchanged. normalize=:unit_effect scales the shock so the impact on shock_size = (variable => value) equals value (variable is an index or name). Bands and FEVD that share Q are rescaled the same way.

CI types

  • :none
  • :bootstrap — residual (recursive-design) bootstrap: resamples the estimated residuals, regenerates data from B, and re-estimates the VAR per replication. With stationary_only=true, draws whose companion matrix has |λmax| ≥ 1 are rejected and redrawn. For :sign/:narrative this mixes Haar rotations with sampling uncertainty and throws unless set_inference=:bootstrap_x_rotations.
  • :theoretical — asymptotic (delta-method) confidence intervals. Throws for :sign/:narrative: identified-set methods do not have theoretical residual-free coefficient bands; use the default identified-set bands or set_inference=:bootstrap_x_rotations.
  • :identified_set — returned automatically for :sign/:narrative: pointwise median over accepted Haar rotations, with quantile bands over that set.

Set-ID options (SID-05)

  • max_draws::Int=1000 — Haar draws for :sign/:narrative (forwarded to compute_Q).
  • set_inference::Symbol=:none — pass :bootstrap_x_rotations to opt into bootstrap×rotation bands with ci_type=:bootstrap.

Bootstrap options ([T271])

  • bootstrap::Symbol=:iid — residual resampling scheme. :iid resamples rows with replacement (the historical default); :wild multiplies each residual ROW by one scalar draw, preserving the contemporaneous cross-equation covariance while randomising the conditional variance (Gonçalves & Kilian 2004); :block concatenates contiguous blocks, retaining serial dependence (Brüggemann et al. 2016).
  • block_length::Int=0 — moving-block length; 0 selects ⌈T^{1/3}⌉.
  • wild_dist::Symbol=:rademacher — :rademacher (±1) or :mammen (matches the third moment as well).
  • bias_correct::Bool=false — Kilian (1998) bootstrap-after-bootstrap. An inner bootstrap estimates the small-sample bias Ψ = E[B*] − B̂; the DGP is re-centred at B̂ − δΨ with Kilian's stationarity shrinkage, each outer draw is corrected by the same Ψ before its IRF is computed, and the reported point IRF is computed from the bias-corrected coefficients (B̂ − δΨ). Off by default, so existing bands/points are unchanged.
  • bias_reps::Int=reps — replications for the inner bias bootstrap.
source
MacroEconometricModels.lp_irf — Method
lp_irf(Y::AbstractMatrix, shock_var::Int, horizon::Int; kwargs...) -> LPImpulseResponse

Convenience function: estimate LP and extract IRF in one call.

source
MacroEconometricModels.lp_irf — Method
lp_irf(model::LPModel{T}; conf_level=DEFAULT_CI_MACRO, ci_type=:analytical, bootstrap=:wild, …)
    -> LPImpulseResponse{T}

Extract impulse response functions with confidence intervals from an LP model.

Confidence intervals

  • ci_type=:analytical (default) — HAC (Newey-West or White) standard errors from the estimated vcov, unchanged from before.
  • ci_type=:bootstrap — percentile bands from a fixed-design residual bootstrap ([T271]). At each horizon the regressor matrix is held fixed and only the errors are resampled: y* = X_h·β̂_h + u*, refit by OLS. Holding the design fixed is what makes this valid for LP, where the regressors are predetermined but the errors are MA(h)-correlated by construction.

Bootstrap options

  • bootstrap::Symbol=:wild — :wild (default here, unlike the VAR, because LP residuals are serially correlated and often heteroskedastic), :block, or :iid.
  • block_length::Int=0 — moving-block length; 0 selects ⌈T^{1/3}⌉.
  • wild_dist::Symbol=:rademacher — or :mammen.
  • reps::Int=500, rng/seed for reproducibility.

The point estimates and standard errors are the analytical ones in both cases; only the bands change, so switching ci_type never moves the reported IRF.

source
MacroEconometricModels.reproduce — Method
reproduce(ir::ImpulseResponse, model::VARModel) -> ReproReport

Re-run the bootstrap that produced ir's confidence bands from the manifest's recorded seed and settings and check the point IRF and bands match bit-for-bit. The source model is passed explicitly — a large object deliberately not retained on the IRF result. Requires ir to have been produced by irf(model, H; ci_type=:bootstrap, seed=N).

model = estimate_var(Y, 2)
ir = irf(model, 20; ci_type=:bootstrap, reps=200, seed=20260717)
reproduce(ir, model)   # ReproReport: PASS
source

MacroEconometricModels.generalized_fevd — Method
generalized_fevd(post::BVARPosterior, horizon; normalize=false, quantiles=[0.16,0.5,0.84],
                 point_estimate=:mean, shock_names=nothing, max_draws=1000,
                 threaded=false) → BayesianFEVD

Bayesian generalized FEVD (Pesaran & Shin 1998) with posterior credible bands.

Each posterior draw of (B, Σ) gives one generalized decomposition; the reported bands are posterior quantiles across draws. Because the decomposition needs no orthogonalization, the method/check_func machinery of the structural fevd does not apply — every draw contributes, and there is no rotation to accept or reject.

See generalized_fevd(::VARModel, ::Int) for the estimand and for what normalize does and does not mean.

source
MacroEconometricModels.generalized_fevd — Method
generalized_fevd(model::VARModel, horizon; normalize=false, shock_names=nothing) → FEVD

Generalized forecast-error variance decomposition (Pesaran & Shin 1998).

The structural fevd accumulates squared orthogonalized IRFs, so it is a proper variance decomposition only when the impact matrix satisfies PP' = Σ, and under a Cholesky identification it depends on the variable ordering. The generalized decomposition instead uses the reduced-form Σ directly: it needs no orthogonalization and is invariant to the variable ordering, which makes it the standard choice when the ordering is arbitrary or a structural identification is unavailable.

The price is that the generalized shocks are correlated, so the shares of a given variable do not sum to one.

Keyword Arguments

  • normalize::Bool = false — rescale each row to sum to one. This is the common applied convention (and what the Diebold-Yilmaz connectedness literature uses), but it is a convention, not an identity: the raw shares genuinely do not decompose the variance into exclusive parts. The unnormalized row sums are recoverable from the returned decomposition and the model's own forecast error variance.
  • shock_names — labels for the shock dimension (defaults to the variable names, since a "shock" here is a shock to a variable, not to an orthogonalized structural factor)

Returns

An FEVD; proportions[i, j, h] is the share of the h-step forecast error variance of variable i attributable to a shock to variable j.

Examples

g = generalized_fevd(model, 20)                  # raw shares, rows need not sum to 1
gn = generalized_fevd(model, 20; normalize=true) # rows sum to 1 by construction

See also fevd.

References

  • Pesaran, H. H., & Shin, Y. (1998). Generalized impulse response analysis in linear multivariate models. Economics Letters, 58(1), 17–29.
source

MacroEconometricModels.historical_decomposition — Method
historical_decomposition(model::VARModel, horizon; method=:cholesky, ...) -> HistoricalDecomposition

Compute historical decomposition for a VAR model.

Decomposes observed data into contributions from each structural shock plus initial conditions.

Arguments

  • model::VARModel: Estimated VAR model
  • horizon::Int: Maximum horizon for MA coefficient computation (typically T_eff)

Keyword Arguments

  • method::Symbol=:cholesky: Identification method
  • check_func=nothing: Sign restriction check function (for method=:sign or :narrative)
  • narrative_check=nothing: Narrative restriction check function (for method=:narrative)
  • max_draws::Int=1000: Maximum draws for sign/narrative identification
  • transition_var=nothing: Transition variable (for method=:smooth_transition)
  • regime_indicator=nothing: Regime indicator (for method=:external_volatility)
  • instruments: Instrument vector/matrix for method=:proxy (length T or T_eff)
  • pattern: SVARPattern for method=:ab
  • target: Variable index or name for method=:max_share
  • horizons / band: Time- or frequency-domain window for :max_share

Methods

:cholesky, :sign, :narrative, :long_run, :proxy, :ab, :max_share, :svec, :fastica, :jade, :sobi, :dcov, :hsic, :student_t, :mixture_normal, :pml, :skew_normal, :nongaussian_ml, :markov_switching, :garch, :smooth_transition, :external_volatility

Note: :smooth_transition requires transition_var kwarg. :external_volatility requires regime_indicator kwarg. :proxy requires instruments and is partial when k < n. :ab requires pattern::SVARPattern. :max_share requires target and is partial; pass horizons or band. Default normalize=:unit_effect; structural shocks are recovered as B₀ \ u when Q is not orthogonal.

For :sign/:narrative, each accepted rotation gets its own HD; the reported contributions, initial conditions, and shocks are the pointwise median. n_effective is the number of accepted rotations. The adding-up identity of the pointwise-median HD is not required (Fry & Pagan 2011).

Returns

HistoricalDecomposition containing:

  • contributions: Shock contributions (Teff × nvars × n_shocks)
  • initial_conditions: Initial condition effects (Teff × nvars)
  • actual: Actual data values
  • shocks: Structural shocks
  • n_effective: Accepted rotations for :sign/:narrative (0 otherwise)

Example

model = estimate_var(Y, 2)
hd = historical_decomposition(model, size(Y, 1) - 2)
verify_decomposition(hd)  # Check decomposition identity
source

The footer appears only when the IRF carries a manifest, which the bootstrap path attaches and ci_type = :none leaves as nothing; irf.manifest holds the same information and stays readable regardless of this keyword.

MacroEconometricModels._lp_robust_vcov — Method
_lp_robust_vcov(X_h, U_h, cov_estimator, h)

Horizon-aware robust variance-covariance for LP regressions.

LP residuals at horizon h have MA(h-1) serial correlation (Jordà 2005), so the Newey-West bandwidth must be at least h+1. When automatic bandwidth selection (bandwidth == 0) yields a smaller value, this function enforces the floor max(m̂_NW, h+1).

For non-NW estimators (White, Driscoll-Kraay), falls through to the standard compute_block_robust_vcov.

MacroEconometricModels.structural_lp — Method
structural_lp(Y::AbstractMatrix{T}, horizon::Int;
              method=:cholesky, lags=4, var_lags=nothing,
              cov_type=:newey_west, conf_level=DEFAULT_CI_MACRO,
              ci_type=:none, reps=200,
              check_func=nothing, narrative_check=nothing,
              max_draws=1000,
              transition_var=nothing, regime_indicator=nothing) -> StructuralLP{T}

Estimate structural LP impulse responses using VAR-based identification with LP estimation.

Algorithm:

  1. Estimate VAR(p) → obtain Σ and residuals
  2. Compute rotation matrix Q via chosen identification method
  3. Compute structural shocks εt = Q'L⁻¹ut
  4. For each shock j, run LP with εj as regressor and Yeff as responses
  5. Stack into 3D IRF array: irfs[h, i, j]

Arguments

  • Y: T × n data matrix
  • horizon: Maximum IRF horizon H

Keyword Arguments

  • method: Identification method
  • lags: Number of LP control lags (default: 4)
  • var_lags: VAR lag order for identification (default: same as lags)
  • cov_type: HAC estimator (:newey_west, :white)
  • conf_level: Confidence level for CIs (default: 0.68, DEFAULT_CI_MACRO)
  • ci_type: CI method (:none, :bootstrap)
  • reps: Number of bootstrap replications
  • check_func: Sign restriction check function (for :sign/:narrative)
  • narrative_check: Narrative check function (for :narrative)
  • max_draws: Maximum draws for sign/narrative identification
  • transition_var: Transition variable (for :smooth_transition)
  • regime_indicator: Regime indicator (for :external_volatility)

Methods

:cholesky, :sign, :narrative, :long_run, :fastica, :jade, :sobi, :dcov, :hsic, :student_t, :mixture_normal, :pml, :skew_normal, :nongaussian_ml, :markov_switching, :garch, :smooth_transition, :external_volatility

Note: :smooth_transition requires transition_var kwarg. :external_volatility requires regime_indicator kwarg.

Returns

StructuralLP{T} with 3D IRFs, structural shocks, VAR model, and individual LP models.

References

Plagborg-Møller, M. & Wolf, C. K. (2021). "Local Projections and VARs Estimate the Same Impulse Responses." Econometrica, 89(2), 955–980.

source

MacroEconometricModels.first_stage_regression — Method
first_stage_regression(endog::AbstractVector{T}, instruments::AbstractMatrix{T},
                       controls::AbstractMatrix{T}; h::Int=0) -> NamedTuple

First-stage regression for 2SLS with HAC-robust F-statistic for instrument relevance.

LP-IV Weak-Instrument-Robust Inference

MacroEconometricModels.montiel_olea_pflueger_f — Function
montiel_olea_pflueger_f(model::LPIVModel; tau=0.10, bandwidth=0) -> MontielOleaPfluegerF

Montiel Olea & Pflueger (2013) effective first-stage F for an LP-IV model.

The classical first-stage F is only a valid weak-instrument diagnostic under homoskedastic, serially uncorrelated errors — precisely what macro data violate. The effective F replaces the homoskedastic scale with a HAR one:

\[F_{\text{eff}} = \frac{\tilde Y_2' P_{\tilde Z} \tilde Y_2} {\operatorname{tr}\!\big(\hat V_{\hat\Pi}\,\tilde Z'\tilde Z\big)}\]

where:

  • $\tilde Z = M_W Z$ are the excluded instruments with the LP controls partialled out
  • $\tilde Y_2 = M_W x$ is the residualized endogenous shock
  • $\hat V_{\hat\Pi}$ is the Newey-West sandwich covariance of the first-stage coefficients

Under homoskedasticity $\hat V_{\hat\Pi} = \hat\sigma_v^2 (\tilde Z'\tilde Z)^{-1}$, the denominator collapses to $q\hat\sigma_v^2$ and $F_{\text{eff}}$ is the classical first-stage F — the reduction Montiel Olea and Pflueger establish.

The LP first stage is the same regression at every horizon (only the sample window shrinks), so the effective F is computed once, at horizon 0.

Keywords

  • tau::Real=0.10 — worst-case relative bias of 2SLS to tolerate (0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.30)
  • bandwidth::Int=0 — HAC lag length; 0 selects it from the data

Returns

MontielOleaPfluegerF.

source
MacroEconometricModels.lp_iv_ar_band — Function
lp_iv_ar_band(model::LPIVModel; level=DEFAULT_CI_MACRO, n_grid=401, span=20, bandwidth=0,
              responses=nothing) -> LPIVARBand

Anderson-Rubin confidence sets for the LP-IV impulse response, horizon by horizon.

At each horizon h and response j, the AR test of H₀: θ_{h,j} = θ₀ regresses Y_{t+h,j} − θ₀ x_t on the controls and instruments and tests the instruments' joint irrelevance. Because that restriction holds under H₀ whatever the first stage looks like, the resulting set has correct coverage no matter how weak the instrument is.

The covariance uses Newey-West with a lag length that scales with the horizon — max(data-driven bandwidth, h+1), the same rule estimate_lp_iv applies to its own standard errors — because the horizon-h LP residuals are MA(h) correlated by construction. The data-driven part is taken from each cell's own LP-IV residuals, and the bandwidth actually used is returned in bandwidths ((H+1) × n_resp).

Sets that reach the edge of the search range are reported as unbounded rather than truncated; the search range spans θ̂ ± span·se around the 2SLS estimate at each cell.

Keywords

  • level::Real=0.68 — nominal coverage (DEFAULT_CI_MACRO)
  • n_grid::Int=401 — grid points per cell
  • span::Real=20 — half-width of the search range, in 2SLS standard errors
  • bandwidth::Int=0 — fixed HAC lag length; 0 uses max(auto, h+1)
  • responses::Union{Nothing,Vector{Int}} — subset of model.response_vars positions to compute

Returns

LPIVARBand.

source
MacroEconometricModels.MontielOleaPfluegerF — Type
MontielOleaPfluegerF{T}

Effective first-stage F for LP-IV.

Fields

  • f_effective::T — the effective F statistic
  • critical_value::T — MOP simplified critical value at the requested bias target
  • tau::T — the worst-case relative-bias target (0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.30)
  • weak::Bool — true when f_effective < critical_value
  • n_instruments::Int — number of excluded instruments q
  • bandwidth::Int — HAC lag length used for the first-stage covariance
  • f_naive::T — the model's stored first-stage F at horizon 0, for comparison
source
MacroEconometricModels.LPIVARBand — Type
LPIVARBand{T}

Anderson-Rubin confidence sets for the LP-IV impulse response, one per horizon and response variable.

Because an AR set need not be an interval, the full component list is kept in sets; lower/upper give the plottable envelope, carrying ±Inf where a set is unbounded and NaN where it is empty.

Fields

  • lower::Matrix{T} / upper::Matrix{T} — (H+1) × n_resp envelope of the AR set
  • sets::Matrix{Vector{Tuple{T,T}}} — the connected components at each horizon/response
  • bounded::Matrix{Bool} / is_empty::Matrix{Bool} — set shape flags
  • wald_lower::Matrix{T} / wald_upper::Matrix{T} — the 2SLS Wald band, for comparison
  • point::Matrix{T} — the LP-IV point estimates
  • bandwidths::Matrix{Int} — HAC lag length used at each horizon/response (scales with h)
  • horizon::Int, level::T, critical_value::T, df1::Int
  • response_names::Vector{String}, shock_name::String
source

Smooth LP (Barnichon & Brownlees 2019)

MacroEconometricModels._lp_shock_irf_covariance — Method
_lp_shock_irf_covariance(Y, shock_var, horizon, lags, response_vars, cov_estimator, jcol) -> Matrix

Full (horizon+1)×(horizon+1) covariance of the LP shock-coefficient IRF for response column jcol, across horizons. Overlapping LP horizons share future windows and so induce strong cross-horizon correlation (an MA structure in the overlapping residuals). The diagonal reproduces the per-horizon Newey–West beta_se²; the off-diagonals are the Bartlett-HAC cross-covariances of the shock-coefficient influence functions ψ_{h,t} = (XtXinv[shock, :] · x_{h,t}) · u_{h,t}, aligned by absolute time t. Replacing the former diagonal-only covariance fixes the systematically too-narrow smooth-LP bands.

source
MacroEconometricModels._smooth_lp_cv_errors — Method
_smooth_lp_cv_errors(Y, shock_var, horizon; lambda_grid, k_folds, kwargs...) -> Vector

k-fold cross-validation errors, one per λ in lambda_grid. For each fold the smooth LP is fit on the training rows and scored against the HELD-OUT unrestricted-LP point IRF (not against its own fit): mean((lp_irf(estimate_lp(test)).values .- model.irf_values).^2). Folds too small to form the horizon regression score Inf and are excluded; a λ whose folds are all Inf gets Inf.

source

MacroEconometricModels.estimate_smooth_lp — Method
estimate_smooth_lp(Y::AbstractMatrix{T}, shock_var::Int, horizon::Int;
                   degree::Int=3, n_knots::Int=4, lambda::T=T(0.0),
                   lags::Int=4, response_vars::Vector{Int}=collect(1:size(Y,2)),
                   cov_type::Symbol=:newey_west, bandwidth::Int=0) -> SmoothLPModel{T}

Estimate Smooth LP by a two-step "smooth-the-point-IRF" procedure (NOT the one-step Barnichon & Brownlees 2019 penalized regression): fit per-horizon LP point IRFs, then fit a penalized B-spline to them. Inference propagates the FULL cross-horizon covariance of the LP point IRFs (an overlapping-horizon HAC via _lp_shock_irf_covariance), not a per-horizon diagonal, so the reported bands account for the strong correlation between overlapping LP horizons.

Estimate smooth transition parameters (γ, c) by minimizing the horizon-0 state-dependent LP SSR (_state_lp_transition_ssr) over (γ, c) via :nlls (Nelder–Mead in an unconstrained reparameterization) or :grid_search. lags should match the lag order of the downstream estimate_state_lp fit so the objective mirrors it.

MacroEconometricModels.exponential_transition — Method

source
MacroEconometricModels.indicator_transition — Method
indicator_transition(z::AbstractVector{T}, c::T) -> Vector{T}

Sharp indicator transition: F(z) = 1 if z ≥ c, else 0

source
MacroEconometricModels.logistic_transition — Method
logistic_transition(z::AbstractVector{T}, gamma::T, c::T) -> Vector{T}

Logistic transition function, increasing in z (Auerbach–Gorodnichenko convention): F(z) = 1 / (1 + exp(-γ(z - c))). F → 1 for high states (expansion), F → 0 for low states (recession), and F(c) = 0.5. The F-weighted regressor block is therefore the expansion regime and the (1 - F)-weighted block the recession regime.

source

LP Forecasting

MacroEconometricModels.forecast — Method
forecast(lp::LPModel{T}, shock_path::AbstractVector{<:Real};
         ci_method=:analytical, conf_level=DEFAULT_CI_MACRO, n_boot=500) -> LPForecast{T}

Compute direct multi-step LP forecasts given a shock trajectory.

For each horizon h=1,...,H, the forecast uses the LP regression coefficients: ŷ{T+h} = αh + βh·shockh + Γh·controlsT

where controls_T are the last lags observations of Y.

Arguments

  • lp: Estimated LP model
  • shock_path: Vector of length H with assumed future shock values

Keyword Arguments

  • ci_method: :analytical (default), :bootstrap, or :none
  • conf_level: Confidence level (default: 0.68, DEFAULT_CI_MACRO)
  • n_boot: Number of bootstrap replications (default: 500)

Returns

LPForecast{T} with point forecasts, CIs, and standard errors.

source
MacroEconometricModels.forecast — Method
forecast(slp::StructuralLP{T}, shock_idx::Int, shock_path::AbstractVector{<:Real};
         ci_method=:analytical, conf_level=DEFAULT_CI_MACRO, n_boot=500) -> LPForecast{T}

Compute direct multi-step forecast from a structural LP model using a specific orthogonalized shock.

Arguments

  • slp: Structural LP model
  • shock_idx: Index of the structural shock to use (1:n)
  • shock_path: Vector of length H with assumed shock values

Keyword Arguments

  • ci_method: :analytical (default), :bootstrap, or :none
  • conf_level: Confidence level (default: 0.68, DEFAULT_CI_MACRO)
  • n_boot: Number of bootstrap replications (default: 500)

LP-FEVD (Gorodnichenko & Lee 2019)

MacroEconometricModels.fevd — Method
fevd(slp::StructuralLP{T}, horizon::Int; kwargs...) -> LPFEVD{T}

Compute LP-based FEVD for structural LP results. Dispatches to lp_fevd.

See lp_fevd for full documentation.

source
MacroEconometricModels.lp_fevd — Method
lp_fevd(slp::StructuralLP{T}, horizon::Int; kwargs...) -> LPFEVD{T}

Compute LP-based FEVD using the R²-based estimator of Gorodnichenko & Lee (2019).

At each horizon h, the share of variable i's forecast error variance due to shock j is estimated by regressing LP forecast error residuals on structural shock leads z{t+h}, z{t+h-1}, ..., z_t and computing R².

Arguments

  • slp: Structural LP result from structural_lp()
  • horizon: Maximum FEVD horizon (capped at IRF horizon)

Keyword Arguments

  • method: Estimator (:r2, :lpa, :lpb). Default: :r2
  • bias_correct: Apply VAR-based bootstrap bias correction. Default: true
  • n_boot: Number of bootstrap replications. Default: 500
  • conf_level: Confidence level for CIs. Default: 0.68 (DEFAULT_CI_MACRO)
  • var_lags: VAR lag order for bias correction (default: HQIC-selected)

Returns

LPFEVD{T} with raw proportions, bias-corrected values, SEs, and CIs.

Reference

Gorodnichenko, Y. & Lee, B. (2019). "Forecast Error Variance Decompositions with Local Projections." JBES, 38(4), 921–933.

source

Factor Models

Static Factor Model

MacroEconometricModels.estimate_factors — Method
estimate_factors(X, r; standardize=true, blocks=nothing) -> FactorModel

Estimate static factor model Xt = Λ Ft + e_t via Principal Component Analysis, or via block-restricted EM when blocks is provided.

Arguments

  • X: Data matrix (T × N), observations × variables
  • r: Number of factors to extract

Keyword Arguments

  • standardize::Bool=true: Standardize data before estimation
  • blocks::Union{Nothing, Dict{Symbol, Vector{Int}}}=nothing: Block restriction map. When provided, each key is a block (factor) name and the value is a vector of variable indices that load on that factor. Variables not in a block have zero loadings on that factor. The number of blocks must equal r.
  • varnames::Union{Nothing, Vector{String}}=nothing: Names for the N panel variables (defaults to "Var 1", …). Used by report / loadings tables (#538).

Returns

FactorModel containing factors, loadings, eigenvalues, and explained variance. When blocks is provided, block_names is set on the returned model.

Example

X = randn(200, 50)  # 200 observations, 50 variables
fm = estimate_factors(X, 3)  # Extract 3 factors via PCA
r2(fm)  # R² for each variable

# Block-restricted estimation
blocks = Dict(:real => [1,2,3,4,5], :nominal => [6,7,8,9,10])
fm_restricted = estimate_factors(X, 2; blocks=blocks)
source

  • n_boot::Int=1000: Number of bootstrap replications (if ci_method=:bootstrap)

HallinLiskaResult{T}

MacroEconometricModels.OnatskiQTestResult — Type
OnatskiQTestResult{T<:AbstractFloat} <: StatsAPI.HypothesisTest

Result of the Onatski (2009) dynamic factor rank hypothesis test:

\[H_0: q = k_0 \quad \text{vs.} \quad H_1: k_0 < q \le k_1\]

where:

  • q is the true number of pervasive dynamic factors,
  • k_0 is the null factor rank (k0 >= 0),
  • k_1 is the alternative upper bound (k1 > k0).

Fields

  • statistic::T: Test statistic $\hat{R}$ (median across frequency blocks in $(0, \pi)$)
  • pvalue::T: Asymptotic right-tail p-value under the complex $TW_2$ spacing-ratio law
  • crit_vals::Vector{T}: Critical values [cv_10, cv_05, cv_01] at 10%, 5%, and 1% levels
  • alpha_levels::Vector{T}: Significance levels [0.10, 0.05, 0.01]
  • k0::Int: Null hypothesis number of dynamic factors
  • k1::Int: Alternative hypothesis maximum number of dynamic factors
  • n_obs::Int: Number of time observations $T$
  • n_vars::Int: Number of cross-sectional series $N$
  • bandwidth::Int: Bartlett lag-window truncation parameter $M$
  • n_blocks::Int: Number of disjoint frequency blocks in $(0, \pi)$
  • rejected_05::Bool: true if pvalue < 0.05 (statistic > crit_vals[2])
source
MacroEconometricModels.AhnHorensteinResult — Type
AhnHorensteinResult{T<:AbstractFloat}

Result of the Ahn & Horenstein (2013) Eigenvalue Ratio (ER) and Growth Ratio (GR) estimators for the number of static factors.

Fields

  • eigenvalues::Vector{T}: Descending sample covariance eigenvalues $\hat{\mu}_1 \ge \dots \ge \hat{\mu}_N$
  • mock_eigenvalue::T: Mock zero-factor eigenvalue $\hat{\mu}_0 = V(0) / \ln(\min(N, T))$
  • er::Vector{T}: Eigenvalue Ratio criterion $ER(k)$ for k = 0:k_max (length k_max + 1)
  • gr::Vector{T}: Growth Ratio criterion $GR(k)$ for k = 0:k_max (length k_max + 1)
  • r_er::Int: Estimated number of static factors maximizing ER(k)
  • r_gr::Int: Estimated number of static factors maximizing GR(k)
  • k_max::Int: Maximum candidate number of factors considered
  • n_obs::Int: Number of time observations $T$
  • n_vars::Int: Number of cross-sectional series $N$
source
MacroEconometricModels.OnatskiEDResult — Type
OnatskiEDResult{T<:AbstractFloat}

Result of the Onatski (2010) Edge Distribution (ED) estimator for the number of static factors via iterative threshold calibration near the bulk spectral edge.

Fields

  • eigenvalues::Vector{T}: Descending sample covariance eigenvalues $\hat{\mu}_1 \ge \dots \ge \hat{\mu}_N$
  • r_ed::Int: Estimated number of static factors at fixed-point convergence
  • delta::T: Final edge threshold $\hat{\delta} = 2|\hat{\beta}|$
  • iterations::Int: Number of fixed-point iterations executed
  • converged::Bool: true if j_{t+1} == j_t within max_iter steps
  • k_max::Int: Maximum candidate number of factors considered
  • n_obs::Int: Number of time observations $T$
  • n_vars::Int: Number of cross-sectional series $N$

MacroEconometricModels.bai_ng_q — Method
bai_ng_q(X, r; p=1, δ=0.1, m=1, standardize=true) -> BaiNgQResult

Bai–Ng (2007) rank statistics on the residual covariance of a VAR(p) in r static PCA factors. $D_{1,k} = (\lambda_{k+1}^2 / \sum_j \lambda_j^2)^{1/2}$ and $D_{2,k} = (\sum_{j>k} \lambda_j^2 / \sum_j \lambda_j^2)^{1/2}$; $\hat q$ is the smallest $k$ with the statistic below $m / \min(N^{1/2-\delta}, T^{1/2-\delta})$.

MacroEconometricModels.estimate_gdfm — Method
estimate_gdfm(X, q; standardize=true, bandwidth=0, kernel=:bartlett, r=0,
              varnames=nothing, spectral=:lag_window,
              allow_missing=false, em_max_iter=50, em_tol=1e-4,
              n_lags_proj=0) -> GeneralizedDynamicFactorModel

Estimate a Generalized Dynamic Factor Model using spectral principal components (Forni, Hallin, Lippi & Reichlin, 2000, 2005), with optional EM-spectral imputation for missing or ragged-edge observations and multi-lag stacked one-sided projection:

\[X_t = \chi_t + \xi_t, \qquad F_t^{(s)} = Z^{\top} X_t^{(s)}, \qquad X_t^{(s)} = [X_t^{\top}, X_{t-1}^{\top}, \dots, X_{t-s}^{\top}]^{\top}\]

where:

  • $X_t$ is the $N \times 1$ observed panel vector at time $t$
  • $\chi_t$ is the $N \times 1$ common component driven by $q$ dynamic shocks
  • $\xi_t$ is the $N \times 1$ weakly correlated idiosyncratic component
  • $s$ is the stacked projection lag depth (n_lags_proj)
  • $X_t^{(s)}$ is the $N(s + 1) \times 1$ stacked panel vector
  • $Z$ is the $N(s + 1) \times r$ one-sided generalized eigenvector weight matrix
  • $F_t^{(s)}$ is the $r \times 1$ one-sided static factor vector

Arguments

  • X: Data matrix (T_obs × N)
  • q: Number of dynamic factors (1 ≤ q ≤ min(T_obs, N))

Keyword Arguments

  • standardize::Bool=true: Standardize data prior to spectral estimation
  • bandwidth::Int=0: Lag truncation M under :lag_window (default max(3, round(0.5√T))), or frequency-ordinate bandwidth under :smoothed_periodogram (default max(3, round(T^{1/3})))
  • kernel::Symbol=:bartlett: Spectral kernel (:bartlett, :parzen, or :tukey)
  • r::Int=0: Number of static factors (0 defaults to q)
  • varnames::Union{Nothing,Vector{String}}=nothing: Names for the N panel variables (defaults to "Var 1", …). Forwarded by the TimeSeriesData dispatch
  • spectral::Symbol=:lag_window: :lag_window (FHLR 2000/2005) or :smoothed_periodogram (legacy)
  • allow_missing::Bool=false: Allow NaN entries (interior or ragged-edge) and impute via cross-sectional EM-PCA warm-start followed by EM-spectral iteration (Inf/-Inf and all-NaN rows or columns are rejected)
  • em_max_iter::Int=50: Maximum EM iterations when allow_missing=true and X contains NaN
  • em_tol::Real=1e-4: Relative convergence tolerance on missing-cell updates $\max |\Delta \hat{\chi}_{\text{missing}}|$
  • n_lags_proj::Int=0: Number of past lags s included in the stacked FHLR one-sided projection (0 uses contemporaneous weights only)

Returns

GeneralizedDynamicFactorModel with two-sided common/idiosyncratic components (in-sample FHLR 2000) and one-sided weights Z, factors_onesided, common_component_onesided (FHLR 2005). When allow_missing=true, model.X stores the converged finite EM-imputed panel so downstream forecast, bandpass_gdfm, and historical_decomposition remain finite. Use forecast(gdfm, h; method=:one_sided) for the projection forecast.

Example

gdfm = estimate_gdfm(X, 3)
common_variance_share(gdfm)  # Fraction of variance explained by common component
source
MacroEconometricModels.forecast — Method
forecast(model::GeneralizedDynamicFactorModel, h; method=:ar, ci_method=:theoretical,
         conf_level=DEFAULT_CI_MACRO, n_boot=1000, rng=Random.default_rng(), seed=nothing,
         n_lags_proj=nothing)

Forecast h steps ahead from an estimated GeneralizedDynamicFactorModel.

Under method=:one_sided or method=:spectral, forecasts follow the FHLR (2005) projection onto the stacked one-sided generalized static factors:

\[\hat{\chi}_{T+1 \mid T} = C_{\chi, 1}^{(s)} Z \left(Z^{\top} \hat{\Gamma}_X^{(s)} Z\right)^{-1} Z^{\top} X_T^{(s)}, \qquad \hat{\chi}_{T+h \mid T} = W_1 \Phi_F^{h-1} F_T^{(s)}\]

where:

  • $\hat{\chi}_{T+h \mid T}$ is the $h$-step-ahead projected common component
  • $s$ is the stacked projection lag depth (n_lags_proj, defaulting to the lag depth in model.Z)
  • $X_T^{(s)} = [X_T^{\top}, X_{T-1}^{\top}, \dots, X_{T-s}^{\top}]^{\top}$ is the stacked panel at the forecast origin
  • $Z$ is the $N(s + 1) \times r$ one-sided generalized eigenvector weight matrix
  • $F_T^{(s)} = Z^{\top} X_T^{(s)}$ is the $r \times 1$ contemporaneous stacked static factor vector
  • $\hat{\Gamma}_X^{(s)}$ is the $N(s + 1) \times N(s + 1)$ block-Toeplitz panel autocovariance
  • $C_{\chi, 1}^{(s)} = [\hat{\Gamma}_{\chi}(1), \dots, \hat{\Gamma}_{\chi}(s + 1)]$ is the one-step common cross-covariance
  • $W_1 = C_{\chi, 1}^{(s)} Z (Z^{\top} \hat{\Gamma}_X^{(s)} Z)^{-1}$ is the one-step projection loading matrix
  • $\Phi_F$ is the stationary VAR(1) transition matrix estimated on centered one-sided factors

Arguments

  • model: Estimated GeneralizedDynamicFactorModel
  • h: Forecast horizon (≥ 1)

Keyword Arguments

  • method::Symbol=:ar: :ar fits an AR(1) on each two-sided factor; :one_sided and :spectral compute the FHLR (2005) projection (:spectral is not an alias for :ar)
  • ci_method::Symbol=:theoretical: Confidence interval method — :none, :theoretical, or :bootstrap. Under :one_sided/:spectral, :theoretical combines common-component projection error $\hat{\Gamma}_\chi(0) - W_h G_z W_h^{\top}$ with idiosyncratic variance (and $h$-step factor projection error for factors_se), and :bootstrap simulates factor VAR(1) innovations alongside resampled idiosyncratic residuals
  • conf_level::Real=0.68: Confidence level for intervals (DEFAULT_CI_MACRO)
  • n_boot::Int=1000: Bootstrap replications (for :bootstrap)
  • rng::AbstractRNG=Random.default_rng(): Random number generator for bootstrap draws
  • seed::Union{Integer,Nothing}=nothing: Optional integer seed overriding rng via Xoshiro(seed)
  • n_lags_proj::Union{Nothing,Int}=nothing: Lag depth s for stacked FHLR :one_sided/:spectral projection (nothing uses the lag depth stored in model.Z, which is 0 by default)

Returns

FactorForecast with factor and observable forecasts (and CIs if requested).

source
MacroEconometricModels.hallin_liska — Method
hallin_liska(X, q_max; c_grid, subpanels=4, bandwidth=0, kernel=:bartlett,
             penalty=:p1, spectral=:lag_window, standardize=true) -> HallinLiskaResult

Hallin–Liška (2007) information criterion for the number of dynamic factors.

$IC(q; c) = \log((1/N)\sum_{j>q}\bar\lambda_j) + q · c · p(N,T)$ is minimised on a grid of $c$. Nested sub-panels $(N_j, T_j)$ give $S_c = \mathrm{sd}_j(\hat q_j(c))$. The second interval with $S_c = 0$ is the estimate; the first plateau near $c = 0$ is $q_{max}$ and is discarded.

Keyword Arguments

  • c_grid: Tuning constants (default range(0, 3, length=100))
  • subpanels::Int=4: Nested $(N_j, T_j)$ panels, largest first
  • penalty::Symbol=:p1: :p1, :p2, or :p3 (Hallin–Liška §4)
  • spectral::Symbol=:lag_window: Must be the FHLR lag-window for the theorem
source
MacroEconometricModels.historical_decomposition — Method
historical_decomposition(gdfm::GeneralizedDynamicFactorModel; by=:dynamic_pc) -> HistoricalDecomposition

Decompose each series into the q dynamic principal-component paths plus the idiosyncratic remainder via finite-lag symmetric time-domain filtering $\hat{\chi}_t^{(j)} = \sum_{k=-M}^{M} K_k^{(j)} X_{t-k}$ with reflection boundary padding. The q paths reconstruct gdfm.common_component (including the series mean under standardize=true, matching estimate_gdfm).

source
MacroEconometricModels.ic_criteria_gdfm — Method
ic_criteria_gdfm(X, max_q; standardize=true, bandwidth=0, kernel=:bartlett)

Eigenvalue-ratio and 90%-variance heuristics for the number of dynamic factors.

These are not consistent estimators of $q$. Prefer hallin_liska, bai_ng_q, or amengual_watson_q. When the 90% threshold is never reached, q_variance == max_q and boundary=true; a warning is emitted.

Returns

Named tuple with eigenvalue_ratios, cumulative_variance, avg_eigenvalues, q_ratio, q_variance, and boundary.

source
MacroEconometricModels.nowcast_gdfm — Method
nowcast_gdfm(gdfm::GeneralizedDynamicFactorModel{T}, X_ragged::AbstractMatrix{<:Real},
             h::Int=0; n_lags::Int=2) where {T<:AbstractFloat} -> GDFMNowcastResult{T}

Compute exact minimum-MSE linear Wiener–Kolmogorov nowcasts (h = 0), backcasts (h < 0), or short-horizon forecasts (h > 0) on a ragged-edge panel X_ragged (T_r × N) using spectral autocovariances from a fitted GeneralizedDynamicFactorModel:

\[\hat{\chi}_{T_r + h \mid T_r} = C_{\chi, \text{obs}} \Sigma_{\text{obs}, \text{obs}}^{-1} X_{\text{obs}}, \qquad \text{SE}_i = \sigma_i \sqrt{\max\!\left(\left(\hat{\Gamma}_{\chi}(0) - C_{\chi, \text{obs}} \Sigma_{\text{obs}, \text{obs}}^{-1} C_{\text{obs}, \chi}\right)_{ii},\, 0\right) + \max\!\left(\hat{\Gamma}_{\xi}(0)_{ii},\, 0\right)}\]

where:

  • $\hat{\chi}_{T_r + h \mid T_r}$ is the conditional expectation of the common component at $T_r + h$ in standardized space
  • $X_{\text{obs}}$ collects all finite entries in $X_{\text{ragged}}[\max(1, T_r - \text{n\_lags}):T_r, :]$ after applying gdfm standardization
  • $\Sigma_{\text{obs}, \text{obs}}$ is the spectral autocovariance matrix of $X_{\text{obs}}$ built from $\hat{\Gamma}_X(k)$
  • $C_{\chi, \text{obs}} = C_{\text{obs}, \chi}^{\top}$ is the cross-covariance between $\chi_{T_r + h}$ and $X_{\text{obs}}$ built from $\hat{\Gamma}_{\chi}(k)$
  • $\hat{\Gamma}_{\chi}(0)$ and $\hat{\Gamma}_{\xi}(0) = \hat{\Gamma}_X(0) - \hat{\Gamma}_{\chi}(0)$ are the lag-0 common and idiosyncratic covariance matrices
  • $\sigma_i$ is the series scaling factor (std(gdfm.X[:, i]) if gdfm.standardized, else 1)

Arguments

  • gdfm::GeneralizedDynamicFactorModel{T}: Pre-estimated GDFM supplying spectral densities and scaling
  • X_ragged::AbstractMatrix{<:Real}: Ragged-edge panel (T_r × N) where NaN marks missing observations (Inf/-Inf rejected)
  • h::Int=0: Horizon relative to T_r (0 = nowcast, h > 0 = forecast, h < 0 = backcast)

Keyword Arguments

  • n_lags::Int=2: Number of past lags included in the conditioning window [max(1, T_r - n_lags), T_r] (≥ 0)

Returns

GDFMNowcastResult storing unstandardized nowcast, se, conditional-expectation panel_imputed, horizon, n_lags, observed_mask, and varnames.

source
MacroEconometricModels.spectral_eigenvalue_plot_data — Method
spectral_eigenvalue_plot_data(model::GeneralizedDynamicFactorModel)

MacroEconometricModels.estimate_gdfm_fhlz — Method
estimate_gdfm_fhlz(X, q; kwargs...) -> FHLZModel

Estimate the one-sided infinite-dimensional Generalized Dynamic Factor Model of Forni, Hallin, Lippi & Zaffaroni (2015, 2017) using random-permutation blockwise Yule–Walker VARs, with optional EM missing-data and ragged-edge imputation:

\[X_t = \chi_t + \xi_t, \qquad \chi_t = \sum_{h=0}^{\infty} B_h u_{t-h}\]

where:

  • $X_t$ is the $N \times 1$ observed panel vector at time $t$
  • $\chi_t$ is the $N \times 1$ one-sided common component
  • $\xi_t$ is the $N \times 1$ weakly correlated idiosyncratic component
  • $B_h$ is the $N \times q$ impulse-response filter matrix at lag $h$
  • $u_t$ is the $q \times 1$ vector of orthonormal dynamic common innovations

Arguments

  • X::AbstractMatrix: Panel data matrix (T_obs × N)
  • q::Int: Number of dynamic common shocks (1 ≤ q ≤ min(T_obs, N))

Keyword Arguments

  • bandwidth::Int=0: Spectral lag-window truncation M (0 uses max(3, round(0.5√T)))
  • kernel::Symbol=:bartlett: Spectral kernel (:bartlett, :parzen, :tukey)
  • p_max::Int=5: Maximum block VAR lag order (≥ 1)
  • lag_criterion::Symbol=:bic: Block lag selection criterion (:bic or :aic)
  • n_perm::Int=30: Number of cross-sectional permutations to average (≥ 1)
  • irf_horizon::Int=40: Number of one-sided MA coefficients B̂_0..B̂_{H-1} (≥ 1)
  • standardize::Bool=true: Standardize series before estimation, then unstandardize outputs
  • threaded::Bool=true: Parallelize across permutations using Threads.@threads
  • varnames::Union{Nothing,Vector{String}}=nothing: Panel variable names (length N)
  • seed::Union{Integer,Nothing}=nothing: Optional RNG seed recorded in manifest
  • rng::AbstractRNG=Random.default_rng(): RNG for permutation draws
  • allow_missing::Bool=false: Allow NaN entries (interior or ragged-edge) and impute via cross-sectional EM-PCA warm-start followed by EM-FHLZ iteration (Inf/-Inf and all-NaN rows or columns are rejected)
  • em_max_iter::Int=50: Maximum EM iterations when allow_missing=true and X contains NaN
  • em_tol::Real=1e-4: Relative convergence tolerance on missing-cell updates $\max |\Delta \hat{\chi}_{\text{missing}}|$

Returns

FHLZModel storing one-sided common/idiosyncratic components, orthonormal innovations, impulse filters, and (when allow_missing=true) the converged finite EM-imputed panel in model.X.

source

MacroEconometricModels.ahn_horenstein — Method
ahn_horenstein(X, k_max=min(10, min(size(X)...) - 2);
               demean=true, standardize=true) -> AhnHorensteinResult

Estimate the number of static factors in a large panel $X$ ($T \times N$) using the Ahn & Horenstein (2013) Eigenvalue Ratio (ER) and Growth Ratio (GR) criteria:

\[ER(k) = \frac{\hat{\mu}_k}{\hat{\mu}_{k+1}}, \qquad GR(k) = \frac{\ln(1 + \hat{\mu}_k / V(k))}{\ln(1 + \hat{\mu}_{k+1} / V(k+1))}, \qquad k = 0, \dots, k_{\max}\]

where:

  • ER(k) is the Eigenvalue Ratio criterion at candidate rank k,
  • GR(k) is the Growth Ratio criterion at candidate rank k,
  • k_{\max} is the maximum number of candidate static factors (1 <= k_{\max} <= min(N, T) - 2),
  • \hat{\mu}_j is the j-th largest eigenvalue of (X' X) / T,
  • \hat{\mu}_0 = V(0) / \ln(\min(N, T)) is the mock eigenvalue for r = 0,
  • V(k) = \sum_{j=k+1}^{\min(N, T)} \hat{\mu}_j is the remaining eigenvalue tail sum.

Arguments

  • X::AbstractMatrix: Panel data matrix (T x N, observations by variables)
  • k_max::Int: Maximum candidate number of static factors (1 <= k_max <= min(N, T) - 2)
  • demean::Bool: Subtract column means before decomposition (default true)
  • standardize::Bool: Standardize columns to unit variance (default true)

Returns

  • AhnHorensteinResult{T} with er, gr, r_er, r_gr, eigenvalues, and mock_eigenvalue.

References

  • Ahn, S. C., & Horenstein, A. R. (2013). Eigenvalue ratio test for the number of factors. Econometrica, 81(3), 1203-1227.
source
MacroEconometricModels.onatski_ed — Method
onatski_ed(X, k_max=min(10, min(size(X)...) - 2);
           max_iter=20, standardize=true, demean=true) -> OnatskiEDResult

Estimate the number of static factors in a large panel $X$ ($T \times N$) using the Onatski (2010) Edge Distribution (ED) fixed-point procedure:

\[\hat{\delta} = 2|\hat{\beta}|, \qquad \hat{r}(\hat{\delta}) = \max\{r \le k_{\max} : \hat{\mu}_r - \hat{\mu}_{r+1} \ge \hat{\delta}\}\]

where:

  • \hat{\beta} is the OLS slope from regressing consecutive eigenvalues \hat{\mu}_j, \dots, \hat{\mu}_{j+4} on a constant and (j - 1)^{2/3}, \dots, (j + 3)^{2/3},
  • \hat{\mu}_r is the r-th largest eigenvalue of (X' X) / T,
  • \hat{\delta} is the threshold separating spiked factor eigenvalues from the bulk spectral edge,
  • k_{\max} is the upper bound on candidate static factors.

Iteration starts from $j = k_{\max} + 1$ and updates $j \leftarrow \hat{r}(\hat{\delta}) + 1$ until a fixed point is reached or max_iter iterations complete.

Arguments

  • X::AbstractMatrix: Panel data matrix (T x N, observations by variables)
  • k_max::Int: Maximum candidate number of static factors (1 <= k_max <= min(N, T) - 2)
  • max_iter::Int: Maximum fixed-point iterations (default 20)
  • standardize::Bool: Standardize columns to unit variance (default true)
  • demean::Bool: Subtract column means before decomposition (default true)

Returns

  • OnatskiEDResult{T} with estimated rank r_ed, calibrated threshold delta, iteration count, convergence status, and eigenvalues.

References

  • Onatski, A. (2010). Determining the number of factors from empirical distribution of eigenvalues. Review of Economics and Statistics, 92(4), 1004-1016.
source
MacroEconometricModels.onatski_q_select — Method
onatski_q_select(X; k_max=min(8, size(X, 2) - 2), alpha=0.05, kwargs...)
onatski_q_select(X, k_max; alpha=0.05, kwargs...)

Select the number of pervasive dynamic factors $\hat{q}$ sequentially upward from $k_0 = 0$ to $k_{\max} - 1$ by testing $H_0: q = k_0$ vs. $H_1: k_0 < q \le k_{\max}$ with onatski_q_test until $H_0$ cannot be rejected at significance level alpha.

Arguments

  • X::AbstractMatrix: Panel data matrix (T x N, observations by variables)
  • k_max::Int: Maximum candidate number of dynamic factors (1 <= k_max <= N - 2)
  • alpha::Real: Significance level in (0, 1) for sequential testing (default 0.05)
  • bandwidth::Union{Int,Nothing}: Lag-window bandwidth M passed to onatski_q_test
  • n_blocks::Union{Int,Nothing}: Number of interior frequency blocks in (0, pi)
  • kernel::Symbol: Lag-window kernel (default :bartlett)
  • freq::Union{Real,Nothing}: Optional single frequency omega in (0, pi)
  • standardize::Bool: Standardize columns to unit variance (default true)
  • demean::Bool: Subtract column means (default true)
  • n_sim::Int: GUE draws when w > 15 (default 5000)
  • rng::AbstractRNG: RNG for the w > 15 fallback
  • seed::Union{Integer,Nothing}: Seed for the w > 15 fallback

Returns

  • (q_hat::Int, tests::Vector{OnatskiQTestResult{T}}) where q_hat is the selected dynamic factor rank and tests contains each sequential test executed.

References

  • Onatski, A. (2009). Testing hypotheses about the number of factors in large factor models. Econometrica, 77(5), 1447-1479.
source
MacroEconometricModels.onatski_q_test — Method
onatski_q_test(X, k0, k1; bandwidth=nothing, n_blocks=nothing,
               kernel=:bartlett, freq=nothing, standardize=true,
               demean=true, n_sim=5000, rng=Random.default_rng(),
               seed=nothing) -> OnatskiQTestResult

Test the number of pervasive dynamic factors $q$ in a large panel $X$ ($T \times N$) for $H_0: q = k_0$ against $H_1: k_0 < q \le k_1$ under Onatski (2009) using the maximum adjacent eigenvalue-gap ratio of smoothed Hermitian spectral density matrices on disjoint interior Fourier frequency blocks in $(0, \pi)$:

\[\hat{R}_b(k_0, k_1) = \max_{k_0 < i \le k_1} \frac{\hat{\lambda}_{i,b} - \hat{\lambda}_{i+1,b}}{\hat{\lambda}_{i+1,b} - \hat{\lambda}_{i+2,b}}, \qquad \hat{R} = \operatorname{median}_{1 \le b \le B} \hat{R}_b(k_0, k_1)\]

where:

  • \hat{R}_b(k_0, k_1) is the block-level maximum eigenvalue spacing ratio on block b,
  • \hat{R} is the overall Onatski (2009) test statistic (median across blocks),
  • k_0 is the null hypothesis dynamic factor rank (k0 >= 0),
  • k_1 is the upper bound under the alternative (k0 < k1 <= N - 2),
  • \hat{\lambda}_{i,b} is the i-th largest eigenvalue of the lag-window spectral matrix on frequency block b,
  • B is the number of disjoint frequency blocks in (0, \pi).

Under $H_0$, $\hat{R}_b$ converges in distribution to the complex Tracy-Widom ($TW_2$) ratio distribution tabulated for $w = k_1 - k_0 \in 1:15$, with a seeded Dumitriu-Edelman GUE tridiagonal simulation fallback for $w > 15$.

Arguments

  • X::AbstractMatrix: Panel data matrix (T x N, observations by variables)
  • k0::Int: Null number of dynamic factors (>= 0)
  • k1::Int: Alternative maximum number of dynamic factors (> k0, <= N - 2)
  • bandwidth::Union{Int,Nothing}: Lag-window truncation M; defaults to clamp(round(Int, sqrt(T)), 3, T - 1)
  • n_blocks::Union{Int,Nothing}: Optional number of disjoint frequency blocks in (0, pi)
  • kernel::Symbol: Lag-window kernel (:bartlett, :parzen, :tukey_hanning); default :bartlett
  • freq::Union{Real,Nothing}: Optional single frequency omega in (0, pi) instead of averaging blocks
  • standardize::Bool: Standardize columns to unit variance (default true)
  • demean::Bool: Subtract column means prior to spectral estimation (default true)
  • n_sim::Int: GUE Monte Carlo draws when k1 - k0 > 15 (default 5000)
  • rng::AbstractRNG: Random number generator for the w > 15 fallback
  • seed::Union{Integer,Nothing}: Optional seed for the w > 15 fallback

Returns

  • OnatskiQTestResult{T} containing test statistic, p-value, critical values, and rejection flag.

References

  • Onatski, A. (2009). Testing hypotheses about the number of factors in large factor models. Econometrica, 77(5), 1447-1479.
source
MacroEconometricModels.common_variance_share — Method

Fraction of each variable's level variance explained by the common component.

MacroEconometricModels.estimate_gdfm_i1 — Method
estimate_gdfm_i1(X_levels, q; kwargs...) -> CointegratedGDFM

Estimate a non-stationary $I(1)$ cointegrated Generalized Dynamic Factor Model via the Barigozzi, Lippi & Luciani (2021) blockwise VECM estimator.

The estimator proceeds in four stages:

  1. Detrend X_levels according to det_trend (:none, :constant, or :linear), form stationary first differences $\Delta X_t$, estimate the lag-window spectral density $\hat{\Sigma}_{\Delta X}(\theta)$ and dynamic PCA common spectrum, and reconstruct the initial level common component $\hat{\chi}_t$.
  2. Partition 1:N across n_perm seeded permutations into $(q + 1)$-blocks and estimate a reduced-rank VECM on each block with cointegrating rank $c_b = n_b - n_{\text{permanent}}$ via concentrated Johansen moments.
  3. Filter the detrended levels through the blockwise polynomial $A^{(m)}(L)$, extract the $q$-dimensional orthonormal innovations $\hat{u}_t^{(m)}$, invert each block VECM in levels to obtain level MA impulse responses $B_h^{(m)}$ and long-run impacts via the Granger representation, and average across Procrustes-aligned permutations.
  4. Identify the $q \times q$ structural matrix $B_0$ (:permanent_transitory, :cholesky, :long_run, :sign, or :proxy) and recover structural shocks $\hat{\eta}_t$.

Arguments

  • X_levels::AbstractMatrix: Panel data in levels (T_obs × N)
  • q::Int: Number of dynamic common shocks (1 ≤ q ≤ min(T_obs - 1, N))

Keyword Arguments

  • p_max::Int=4: Maximum block VECM VAR order in levels (≥ 1)
  • n_perm::Int=25: Number of random cross-sectional permutations to average (≥ 1)
  • bandwidth::Int=0: Lag-window truncation M (0 selects max(3, round(0.5√T)))
  • kernel::Symbol=:bartlett: Lag-window kernel (:bartlett, :parzen, or :tukey)
  • det_trend::Symbol=:constant: Deterministic trend (:none, :constant, or :linear)
  • irf_horizon::Int=40: Maximum level IRF horizon H (irf_matrices stores h = 0..H)
  • identification::Symbol=:permanent_transitory: Identification (:permanent_transitory, :cholesky, :long_run, :sign, or :proxy)
  • sign_check::Union{Nothing,Function}=nothing: Predicate on (irf_horizon+1) × N × q IRF array required when identification=:sign
  • n_permanent::Union{Nothing,Int}=nothing: Number of permanent stochastic trends (0..q); nothing selects automatically via Johansen cointegration rank on level PCs
  • order::Union{Nothing,Vector{Int}}=nothing: Observable indices for Cholesky/permanent ordering
  • target_vars::Union{Nothing,Vector{Int},Vector{<:AbstractString}}=nothing: Target series for :long_run
  • instrument::Union{Nothing,AbstractVector}=nothing: External instrument for :proxy
  • normalize::Union{Nothing,Tuple}=nothing: Normalization (var, val) for :proxy
  • max_draws::Int=1000: Maximum Haar draws for :sign search
  • lag_criterion::Symbol=:bic: Block VECM lag selection criterion (:bic or :aic)
  • standardize::Bool=true: Scale each series by first-difference standard deviation prior to PCA
  • threaded::Bool=true: Parallelize across permutations using Threads.@threads
  • varnames::Union{Nothing,AbstractVector{<:AbstractString}}=nothing: Panel variable names (length N)
  • shock_names::Union{Nothing,AbstractVector{<:AbstractString}}=nothing: Structural shock names (length q)
  • rng::AbstractRNG=Random.default_rng(): RNG for permutation and sign draws
  • seed::Union{Integer,Nothing}=nothing: Optional seed recorded in manifest

Returns

CointegratedGDFM{T} containing level common/idiosyncratic components, level IRFs, long-run impact matrix, structural shocks, and diagnostics.

References

  • Barigozzi, M., Lippi, M., & Luciani, M. (2021). Large-Dimensional Dynamic Factor Models: Estimation of Impulse-Response Functions With I(1) Cointegrated Factors. Journal of Business & Economic Statistics, 39(1), 323–333.
source

MacroEconometricModels.bandpass_gdfm — Method
bandpass_gdfm(gdfm::GeneralizedDynamicFactorModel, band; r=gdfm.r) -> BandPassGDFMResult

Extract the frequency-band common component of a GeneralizedDynamicFactorModel over $|\theta| \in [\omega_{\text{low}}, \omega_{\text{high}}] \subseteq [0, \pi]$.

Computes both the two-sided dynamic-PCA band-filtered component (common_twosided) and the strictly one-sided FHLR generalized principal component projection (common_onesided). Partitioning $[0, \pi]$ at any $\omega_0 \in (0, \pi)$ into (0, ω₀) and (ω₀, π) recovers gdfm.common_component additively (common_twosided((0, ω₀)) + common_twosided((ω₀, π)) ≈ gdfm.common_component).

Arguments

  • gdfm::GeneralizedDynamicFactorModel: Estimated Generalized Dynamic Factor Model
  • band::Tuple{Real,Real}: Angular frequency band (omega_low, omega_high) with 0 ≤ omega_low < omega_high ≤ π

Keyword Arguments

  • r::Int=gdfm.r: Static factor rank for one-sided projection (gdfm.q ≤ r ≤ N)

Returns

BandPassGDFMResult containing two-sided and one-sided band common components, band variance shares, and metadata.

MacroEconometricModels.eurocoin_indicator — Method
eurocoin_indicator(X, target, q; cutoff=π/6, r=0, bandwidth=0, kernel=:bartlett, standardize=true) -> EurocoinResult

Construct the Altissimo et al. (2010) real-time Eurocoin low-frequency growth indicator by projecting the medium-to-long-run component of target ($|\theta| \le \text{cutoff}$) onto the strictly one-sided FHLR generalized static factor space $Z^{\top} X_t$.

Because estimation of the factor projection weights uses only contemporaneous $F_t = Z^{\top} X_t$ weighted via the band-integrated cross-spectrum $\int_{-\text{cutoff}}^{\text{cutoff}} \hat{\Sigma}_{F,y}(\theta)\, d\theta$, the resulting indicator suffers no end-of-sample phase shift or two-sided filter distortion at $t = T_{\text{obs}}$.

Arguments

  • X::AbstractMatrix: Panel of monthly/high-frequency indicators (T_obs × N)
  • target::AbstractVector: Target growth series (length T_obs)
  • q::Int: Number of dynamic common shocks (1 ≤ q ≤ min(T_obs, N))

Keyword Arguments

  • cutoff::Real=π/6: Angular frequency low-pass cutoff (default π/6, periods ≥ 12 months)
  • r::Int=0: Static factor dimension for one-sided weights (0 defaults to q)
  • bandwidth::Int=0: Spectral lag-window truncation M (0 selects automatically)
  • kernel::Symbol=:bartlett: Spectral lag-window kernel (:bartlett, :parzen, or :tukey)
  • standardize::Bool=true: Standardize columns of X before spectral and factor extraction

Returns

EurocoinResult containing the real-time indicator, benchmark target_lowpass, projection weights, r2, and model metadata.

References

  • Altissimo, F., Cristadoro, R., Forni, M., Lippi, M., & Veronese, G. (2010). New Eurocoin: Tracking Economic Growth in Real Time. Review of Economics and Statistics, 92(4), 1024–1034.
source

spectral_fevd(sdfm::StructuralDFM, band=(2π/32, 2π/6); include_idiosyncratic=false) -> SpectralFEVDResult

MacroEconometricModels.estimate_tv_gdfm — Method
estimate_tv_gdfm(X, q; kwargs...) -> TimeVaryingGDFM

Estimate a locally stationary time-varying Generalized Dynamic Factor Model via localized kernel-tapered lag-window spectral estimation and dynamic principal component projection (Eichler, Motta & von Sachs 2011; Barigozzi, Hallin, Soccorsi & von Sachs 2021).

At each rescaled time point $u_r \in (0, 1)$, the estimator computes kernel-weighted lag autocovariances using symmetric square-root tapering:

\[\hat{\Gamma}(u_r, k) = \sum_{t=k+1}^{T} \sqrt{w_t(u_r)\, w_{t-k}(u_r)}\, X_t X_{t-k}^{\top}\]

where:

  • $u_r$ is the $r$-th rescaled time grid point in $(0, 1)$
  • $w_t(u_r)$ are normalized time-kernel weights summing to $1$
  • $X_t$ is the (standardized and centered) $N \times 1$ panel vector at time $t$
  • $k$ is the lag index in $0, \dots, M_T$
  • $T$ is the sample size size(X, 1)

The resulting Hermitian local spectral density $\hat{\Sigma}_X(u_r, \theta)$ is decomposed into its top-$q$ dynamic eigenvectors, inverted to local two-sided projector kernels $K_k(u_r)$, linearly interpolated to every observation $t = 1, \dots, T$, and applied with symmetric reflection padding to recover common_component.

Arguments

  • X::AbstractMatrix: Panel data matrix (T_obs × N)
  • q::Int: Number of dynamic common factors (1 ≤ q ≤ min(T_obs, N))

Keyword Arguments

  • time_bandwidth::Real=0.0: Time-kernel bandwidth b_T (0 selects max(0.12, T_obs^(-0.2)))
  • lag_bandwidth::Int=0: Spectral lag-window truncation M_T (0 selects max(3, round(0.5 * sqrt(b_T * T_obs))))
  • time_kernel::Symbol=:epanechnikov: Local time kernel (:epanechnikov, :gaussian, or :uniform)
  • lag_kernel::Symbol=:bartlett: Lag-window kernel (:bartlett, :parzen, or :tukey)
  • n_grid::Int=25: Number of rescaled time grid points in (0, 1) (≥ 1)
  • standardize::Bool=true: Standardize panel columns prior to filtering, then unstandardize outputs
  • varnames::Union{Nothing,AbstractVector{<:AbstractString}}=nothing: Panel variable names (length N)

Returns

TimeVaryingGDFM containing the reconstructed common_component, idiosyncratic remainder, local eigenvalues_path, and time-varying variance_share_path.

Example

X = dgp_dynamic_factors(Xoshiro(42); N=20, T=200).X
tv = estimate_tv_gdfm(X, 2)
common_variance_share(tv)

References

  • Barigozzi, M., Hallin, M., Soccorsi, S., & von Sachs, R. (2021). Time-varying generalized dynamic factor models. Econometric Theory, 37(6), 1152–1205.
  • Eichler, M., Motta, G., & von Sachs, R. (2011). Fitting dynamic factor models to non-stationary time series. Journal of Econometrics, 163(1), 51–70.
source

Quantile Factor Model

MacroEconometricModels.estimate_qfm — Method
estimate_qfm(X, r, tau=0.5; standardize=false, robust_scale=true, init=:pca,
             n_starts=3, max_iter=200, tol=1e-7, threaded=true,
             varnames=nothing, method=:iqr, bandwidth=:auto, kernel=:gaussian,
             se=:none, hac_bandwidth=:auto, n_boot=200,
             rng=Random.default_rng(), seed=nothing) -> QuantileFactorModel

Estimate a Quantile Factor Model (Chen, Dolado & Gonzalo 2021) at quantile level tau using Iterative Quantile Regression (:iqr) or Convolution-Smoothed QFA (:smoothed_iqr).

\[Q_{\tau}(X_{it} \mid f_t) = \lambda_i(\tau)' f_t(\tau)\]

where:

  • $X_{it}$ is the panel observation for unit $i$ at time $t$
  • $\tau \in (0, 1)$ is the quantile level
  • $\lambda_i(\tau) \in \mathbb{R}^r$ is the quantile loading vector for unit $i$
  • $f_t(\tau) \in \mathbb{R}^r$ is the quantile factor vector at time $t$

Arguments

  • X::AbstractMatrix — data panel (T_obs × N)
  • r::Int — number of quantile factors (1 ≤ r ≤ min(T_obs, N))
  • tau::Real = 0.5 — quantile level strictly in (0, 1)

Keyword Arguments

  • standardize::Bool = false — standardize columns before fitting (common component is returned in raw X units)
  • robust_scale::Bool = true — when standardize=true, use median and normalized IQR (with MAD and SD fallbacks) instead of mean and SD
  • init::Symbol = :pca — start-1 initialization (:pca, :quantile_svd, or :random)
  • n_starts::Int = 3 — number of multi-start initializations (starts 2..n_starts use reproducible random orthonormal Gaussian factors)
  • max_iter::Int = 200 — maximum outer alternating IQR or L-BFGS iterations
  • tol::Real = 1e-7 — convergence tolerance on relative objective / gradient norm
  • threaded::Bool = true — parallelize batched quantile regressions and bootstrap draws across threads
  • varnames::Union{Nothing,Vector{String}} = nothing — variable names (default "Var 1", …)
  • method::Symbol = :iqr — :iqr (exact alternating IPM) or :smoothed_iqr (convolution-smoothed L-BFGS)
  • bandwidth::Union{Symbol,Real} = :auto — smoothing bandwidth h for method=:smoothed_iqr (:auto uses max(0.05, std(resid_init) * (log(N * T_obs) / min(N, T_obs))^0.25))
  • kernel::Symbol = :gaussian — smoothing kernel (:gaussian, :logistic, or :epanechnikov)
  • se::Symbol = :none — standard errors (:none, :asymptotic CDG Theorem 1 sandwich, or :bootstrap asymmetric wild quantile bootstrap with Procrustes alignment)
  • hac_bandwidth::Union{Symbol,Int} = :auto — Newey–West Bartlett lag truncation across t for se_loadings (:auto selects via Andrews 1991 plug-in; 0 disables HAC lags)
  • n_boot::Int = 200 — bootstrap replications when se=:bootstrap
  • rng::AbstractRNG = Random.default_rng() — RNG for random starts and bootstrap draws
  • seed::Union{Integer,Nothing} = nothing — integer seed overriding rng via Xoshiro(seed)

Returns

A QuantileFactorModel containing normalized factors (F'F / T = I_r), loadings (Λ'Λ / N = diag(σ₁² ≥ … ≥ σ_r²)), common component, residuals, check-loss objective, Koenker–Machado pseudo-R², optional se_loadings and se_factors, and reproducibility manifest.

Example

X = randn(200, 80)
qfm = estimate_qfm(X, 2, 0.9; method=:smoothed_iqr, se=:asymptotic, seed=42)
report(qfm)

References

  • Chen, L., Dolado, J. J., & Gonzalo, J. (2021). Quantile factor models. Econometrica, 89(2), 875–910.
  • Fernandes, M., Guerre, E., & Horta, E. (2021). Smoothing quantile regressions. Journal of Business & Economic Statistics, 39(1), 338–357.
  • He, Y., Kong, X., Yu, L., & Zhang, X. (2023). Quantile factor analysis for large-dimensional time series with smoothed quantile regression. Journal of Econometrics, 232(2), 367–390.
source
MacroEconometricModels.estimate_qfm — Method
estimate_qfm(X, :auto, tau=0.5; r_max=min(10, max(1, min(size(X)...) - 1)),
             criterion=:rank, kwargs...) -> QuantileFactorModel

Automatically select the quantile factor count r(τ) via ic_criteria_qfm and fit the QuantileFactorModel at the selected rank.

Keyword Arguments

  • r_max::Int = min(10, max(1, min(size(X)...) - 1)) — maximum factor count considered
  • criterion::Symbol = :rank — selection rule (:rank, :ic1, :ic2, or :ic3)
  • kwargs... — forwarded to ic_criteria_qfm and estimate_qfm

MacroEconometricModels.estimate_qfm — Method
estimate_qfm(X, r_or_auto, taus; threaded=true, rng=Random.default_rng(),
             seed=nothing, kwargs...) -> QuantileFactorGrid{T}

Estimate a QuantileFactorGrid of quantile factor models across a vector of quantiles taus.

\[Q_{\tau_j}(X_{it} \mid f_t) = \lambda_i(\tau_j)' f_t(\tau_j), \qquad j = 1, \dots, M\]

where:

  • $X_{it}$ is the panel observation for unit $i$ at time $t$
  • $\tau_j \in (0, 1)$ is the $j$-th quantile level in taus
  • $\lambda_i(\tau_j) \in \mathbb{R}^{r(\tau_j)}$ is the quantile loading vector for unit $i$
  • $f_t(\tau_j) \in \mathbb{R}^{r(\tau_j)}$ is the quantile factor vector at time $t$

Arguments

  • X::AbstractMatrix — data panel (T_obs × N)
  • r_or_auto::Union{Int,Symbol,AbstractVector{<:Integer}} — fixed factor count r, :auto (per-quantile selection via ic_criteria_qfm), or a vector of ranks aligned with taus
  • taus::AbstractVector{<:Real} — non-empty vector of quantile levels strictly in (0, 1)

Keyword Arguments

  • threaded::Bool = true — parallelize across quantiles with pre-seeded per-slot RNGs
  • rng::AbstractRNG = Random.default_rng() — RNG used to derive per-quantile slot seeds
  • seed::Union{Integer,Nothing} = nothing — integer seed overriding rng via Xoshiro(seed)
  • kwargs... — keyword arguments forwarded to single-quantile estimate_qfm

Returns

A QuantileFactorGrid supporting integer indexing grid[j] and keyword lookup grid[tau=0.1].

References

  • Chen, L., Dolado, J. J., & Gonzalo, J. (2021). Quantile factor models. Econometrica, 89(2), 875–910.
source
MacroEconometricModels.ic_criteria_qfm — Method
ic_criteria_qfm(X, r_max, tau=0.5; method=:iqr, threshold_scale=1.0,
                warn_boundary=true, kwargs...) -> QFMICResult

Select the quantile-specific factor count r(τ) using Chen, Dolado & Gonzalo (2021, §3.2, Theorem 2) rank-minimization (r_rank) and Bai–Ng check-loss information criteria (r_IC1–r_IC3).

\[L_{NT} = \min(\sqrt{N}, \sqrt{T}), \qquad P_{NT} = c \, \hat{\sigma}_1^2(\tau) \, L_{NT}^{-2/3} \left(\log L_{NT}^2\right)^{1/3}\]

where:

  • $N$ is the number of panel variables
  • $T$ is the number of time-series observations
  • $L_{NT}$ is the panel rate $\min(\sqrt{N}, \sqrt{T})$
  • $c$ is threshold_scale
  • $\hat{\sigma}_1^2(\tau)$ is the leading normalized factor strength from the r_max fit
  • $P_{NT}$ is the rank-minimization threshold

Arguments

  • X::AbstractMatrix{<:Real} — data panel (T_obs × N)
  • r_max::Int — maximum number of factors to consider (1 ≤ r_max ≤ min(T_obs, N))
  • tau::Real = 0.5 — quantile level strictly in (0, 1)

Keyword Arguments

  • method::Symbol = :iqr — QFM estimation method (:iqr or :smoothed_iqr)
  • threshold_scale::Real = 1.0 — positive multiplier c on the CDG threshold P_NT
  • warn_boundary::Bool = true — emit @warn when r_rank == r_max or r_IC1 == r_max
  • kwargs... — additional keyword arguments forwarded to estimate_qfm (e.g., standardize, robust_scale, init, n_starts, max_iter, tol, threaded, bandwidth, kernel, rng, seed)

Returns

A QFMICResult containing r_rank, r_IC1, r_IC2, r_IC3, sigma_sq, threshold_PNT, loss, IC1, IC2, and IC3.

Example

X = randn(200, 80)
ic = ic_criteria_qfm(X, 5, 0.1; init=:quantile_svd, seed=42)
report(ic)

References

  • Chen, L., Dolado, J. J., & Gonzalo, J. (2021). Quantile factor models. Econometrica, 89(2), 875–910.
  • Bai, J., & Ng, S. (2002). Determining the number of factors in approximate factor models. Econometrica, 70(1), 191–221.
source

report(res::QFMICResult)

MacroEconometricModels.test_qfm_loading_constancy — Method
test_qfm_loading_constancy(grid_or_X, tau1, tau2; r=:auto, n_boot=299,
                           block_length=0, bootstrap=:block, threaded=true,
                           max_iter=60, tol=1e-5, rng=Random.default_rng(),
                           seed=nothing, kwargs...) -> QFMLoadingConstancyTest

Test equality of quantile factor loading subspaces across quantiles tau1 and tau2:

\[H_0: \mathrm{span}(\Lambda(\tau_1)) = \mathrm{span}(\Lambda(\tau_2)), \qquad S = \|(I - P_{\hat{\Lambda}(\tau_2)}) \hat{\Lambda}(\tau_1)\|_F^2\]

where:

  • $\Lambda(\tau_1)$ is the $N \times r_1$ loading matrix at quantile $\tau_1$
  • $\Lambda(\tau_2)$ is the $N \times r_2$ loading matrix at quantile $\tau_2$
  • $P_{\hat{\Lambda}(\tau_2)}$ is the orthogonal projector onto $\mathrm{span}(\hat{\Lambda}(\tau_2))$
  • $S$ is the Frobenius-squared projection residual norm (when $r_1 < r_2$, the higher-rank loadings are projected onto the lower-rank subspace so rank differences are detected symmetrically)

P-values are computed via a recentered moving-block (bootstrap=:block) or wild-panel (bootstrap=:wild) bootstrap with Procrustes rotation alignment.

Arguments

  • grid_or_X — a QuantileFactorGrid containing both tau1 and tau2, or a data panel X (T_obs × N)
  • tau1::Real, tau2::Real — quantile levels strictly in (0, 1)

Keyword Arguments

  • r::Union{Int,Symbol,AbstractVector{<:Integer}} = :auto — factor rank specification when passing a raw matrix X (fixed Int, :auto, or 2-element rank vector [r₁, r₂])
  • n_boot::Int = 299 — number of bootstrap replications
  • block_length::Int = 0 — moving-block length (0 uses ⌈T^{1/3}⌉)
  • bootstrap::Symbol = :block — :block (recentered moving-block) or :wild (recentered wild-panel)
  • threaded::Bool = true — parallelize bootstrap draws across threads with pre-seeded per-slot RNGs
  • max_iter::Int = 60 — maximum IQR iterations per warm-started bootstrap draw
  • tol::Real = 1e-5 — convergence tolerance per bootstrap draw
  • rng::AbstractRNG = Random.default_rng() — RNG for bootstrap resampling
  • seed::Union{Integer,Nothing} = nothing — integer seed overriding rng via Xoshiro(seed)
  • kwargs... — additional keywords forwarded to estimate_qfm when passing X

Returns

A QFMLoadingConstancyTest subtyping StatsAPI.HypothesisTest.

References

  • Chen, L., Dolado, J. J., & Gonzalo, J. (2021). Quantile factor models. Econometrica, 89(2), 875–910.
source
MacroEconometricModels.test_qfm_spanning — Method
test_qfm_spanning(qfm_or_grid, pca_or_X; threshold=0.5, r_pca=:auto,
                  r_max=min(10, max(1, min(size(X)...) - 1)),
                  criterion=:ic2, standardize=nothing) -> QFMSpanningResult

Test whether quantile factors $\hat{F}(\tau)$ are spanned by conditional-mean PCA factors $\hat{F}_{\text{PCA}}$ (Chen, Dolado & Gonzalo 2021, §5 Table III).

\[R^2_k(\tau) = 1 - \frac{\|(I - P_{[1, \hat{F}_{\text{PCA}}]}) \hat{F}_k(\tau)\|_2^2} {\|\hat{F}_k(\tau) - \bar{F}_k(\tau)\|_2^2}, \qquad R^1_k(\tau) = 1 - \frac{\min_\beta \sum_t \rho_\tau(\hat{F}_{kt}(\tau) - [1, \hat{F}_{t,\text{PCA}}]'\beta)} {\min_c \sum_t \rho_\tau(\hat{F}_{kt}(\tau) - c)}\]

where:

  • $\hat{F}_k(\tau)$ is the $k$-th estimated quantile factor at quantile $\tau$
  • $\hat{F}_{\text{PCA}}$ is the $T \times r_{\text{PCA}}$ matrix of conditional-mean PCA factors
  • $P_{[1, \hat{F}_{\text{PCA}}]}$ is the orthogonal projection onto the intercept and PCA factors
  • $R^2_k(\tau)$ is the centered OLS R-squared for quantile factor $k$
  • $R^1_k(\tau)$ is the Koenker–Machado quantile pseudo-R-squared at level $\tau$

Arguments

  • qfm_or_grid — a QuantileFactorModel or QuantileFactorGrid
  • pca_or_X — a FactorModel from estimate_factors, or the data matrix X (omitting pca_or_X uses the panel stored on qfm_or_grid)

Keyword Arguments

  • threshold::Real = 0.5 — OLS $R^2$ cutoff below which a quantile factor is flagged as unspanned
  • r_pca::Union{Int,Symbol} = :auto — number of PCA factors when pca_or_X is a matrix (or omitted); :auto selects via Bai–Ng ic_criteria
  • r_max::Int — upper bound for Bai–Ng PCA factor selection when r_pca == :auto
  • criterion::Symbol = :ic2 — Bai–Ng criterion (:ic1, :ic2, or :ic3) when r_pca == :auto
  • standardize::Union{Bool,Nothing} = nothing — passed to estimate_factors when computing PCA from X; nothing matches qfm.standardized (or grid[1].standardized)

Returns

A QFMSpanningResult reporting ols_r2, quantile_r1, cancor, and unspanned flags.

References

  • Chen, L., Dolado, J. J., & Gonzalo, J. (2021). Quantile factor models. Econometrica, 89(2), 875–910.
  • Koenker, R., & Machado, J. A. F. (1999). Goodness of fit and related inference processes for quantile regression. JASA, 94(448), 1296–1310.
